Remark 11
.

It is not difficult to prove that for any unstable manifold/disk
\gamma^{u}
we have
(\mu_{X})_{\gamma^{u}}\ll\Leb_{\gamma^{u}}
, where
(\mu_{X})_{\gamma^{u}}
is the conditional measure of
\mu_{X}
on an unstable manifold/disk
\gamma^{u}
. This
\mu_{X}
is a Sinai-Ruelle-Bowen measure (
SRB measure
).

Thanks to all preparations above, we can construct now thicker hyperbolic and expanding Young towers.

5.2
Thicker hyperbolic Young towers for
(\Delta_{m},F^{R_{m}},\mu_{\Delta_{m}})

Lemma 8

(\Delta_{m},F^{R_{m}},\mu_{\Delta_{m}})
is K-mixing for sufficiently large
m\geq 1
. (Observe, that it is not true for a small
m
).

Proof.

By Definition
4
,
\gcd\{R\}=1
. Suppose that
\gcd\{i_{1},i_{2},\cdots,i_{k}\}=1
. Then we have
\gcd\{R\}=\gcd\{R|_{\{R=i_{1}\}},R|_{\{R=i_{2}\}},\cdots,R|_{\{R=i_{k}\}}\}=1
. Choose now
m\geq\max\{i_{1},i_{2},\cdots,i_{k}\}
. Then
\gcd\{R_{m}\}

=\gcd\{R\}=1
. Besides,
F^{R_{m}^{\min\{R,m+1\}}}|_{X}=g:X\to X
is K-mixing. Therefore,
(\Delta_{m},F^{R_{m}},\mu_{\Delta_{m}})
is K-mixing, and thus mixing.
∎

From now on we will consider only large enough
m
. Define a new tower (called a thicker hyperbolic Young tower) as

\displaystyle\Delta_{e,m}=\bigcup_{i\geq 1}\bigcup_{0\leq j\leq R^{e}_{i}-1}\Lambda_{i}\times\{j\}\times\{0,1,2,\cdots,m_{i,j}\},

where
m_{i,j}=\min\{m,R\big(g^{j}(x)\big)-1\}
for
0\leq j\leq R^{e}_{i}-1
and any
x\in\Lambda_{i}
.
\Delta_{e,m}
can be visualized as “inserting” extra layers into
\Delta_{e}
. Hence
\Delta_{e.m}
is thicker than
\Delta_{e}
.

Lemma 9

m_{i,j}
is well-defined.

Proof.

To prove this lemma we just need to show that
R\big(g^{j}(x)\big)
does not depend on any
x\in\Lambda_{i}
. It follows from
j\leq R^{e}_{i}-1
that
g^{R^{e}_{i}-j}
is smooth on
g^{j}(\Lambda_{i})
. Therefore
g^{j}(\Lambda_{i})\subseteq\mathbb{S}^{c}
. By Assumption
1
,
\bigcup_{i\geq 1}\partial X_{i}\subseteq\mathbb{S}
. Thus
g^{j}(\Lambda_{i})\subsetneq X_{k}
for some
k\geq 1
. Since
R
is constant on
X_{k}
, then
R(y)=R|_{X_{k}}
for any
y\in g^{j}(\Lambda_{i})
, and
m_{i,j}
is well-defined.
∎

We identify
\Lambda_{i}\times\{j\}\times\{0\}
with
\Lambda_{i}\times\{j\}
, and
\Lambda\times\{0\}\times\{0\}
with
\Lambda
. Then
\Delta_{e}
is a sub-tower of
\Delta_{e,m}
. Define now a map
F_{e,m}:\Delta_{e,m}\to\Delta_{e,m}
, so that for any
x\in\Lambda_{i}
and for some
i\geq 1

\displaystyle F_{e,m}(x,j,k):=\begin{cases}(x,j,k+1),&j\leq R^{e}_{i}-1,k<m_{i,j}\\
(x,j+1,0),&j<R^{e}_{i}-1,k=m_{i,j}\\
\left(g^{R^{e}_{i}}(x),0,0\right),&j=R^{e}_{i}-1,k=m_{i,j}\\
\end{cases}.

The set
\Lambda
, as the base of
\Delta_{e,m}
, has a return time
R_{e,m}|_{\Lambda_{i}}:=\sum_{j<R^{e}_{i}}(1+m_{i,j})
, such that
F_{e,m}^{R_{e,m}}=g^{R^{e}}:\Lambda\to\Lambda
is the induced map for the tower
(\Delta_{e,m},F_{e,m})
.

Define a probability measure
\mu_{\Delta_{e,m}}
on
\Delta_{e,m}
as

\displaystyle\mu_{\Delta_{e,m}}:=(\int R_{e,m}d\mu_{\Lambda})^{-1}\sum_{j}(F_{e,m}^{j})_{*}\big(\mu_{\Lambda}|_{\{R_{e,m}>j\}}\big).

A projection
\pi_{e,m}:\Delta_{e,m}\to\Delta_{m}
is defined by

\displaystyle\pi_{e,m}(x,j,k):=F^{k}\circ g^{j}(x).

(5.7)

Lemma 10

For any
\gamma\in\Gamma^{u}
we have
R^{e}\leq R_{e,m}\leq 2mR^{e}
, and
\Leb_{\gamma}\left(R_{e,m}>n\right)\leq C\beta^{n/(2m)}
.

Proof.

The first inequality is obvious, since
m_{i,j}\leq m
for any
i,j
. By (
5.1
),
\Leb_{\gamma}\left(R_{e,m}>n\right)\leq\Leb_{\gamma}\left(R^{e}>n/2m\right)\leq C\beta^{n/(2m)}
.
∎

Lemma 11

(\pi_{e,m})_{*}\mu_{\Delta_{e,m}}=\mu_{\Delta_{m}}
and
\pi_{e,m}
is a semi-conjugacy, i.e.,
\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}=F^{R_{m}}\circ\pi_{e,m}
.

Proof.

At first we prove a semi-conjugacy. For any
(x,j,k)\in\Delta_{e,m}
, where
x\in\Lambda_{i}
for some
i\geq 1
, suppose that
k<m_{i,j}
,

\displaystyle\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}(x,j,k)=\pi_{e,m}(x,j,k+1)=F^{k+1}\circ g^{j}(x),

\displaystyle F^{R_{m}}\circ\pi_{e,m}(x,j,k)=F^{R_{m}}\circ F^{k}\circ g^{j}(x)=F^{k+1}\circ g^{j}(x),

where the last equality holds because
\pi_{\mathbb{N}}\big(F^{k}\circ g^{i}(x)\big)=k<m_{i,j}
.

Suppose that
k=m_{i,j}
,
j\leq R^{e}_{i}-1
,

\displaystyle\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}(x,j,k)=\pi_{e,m}(x,j+1,0)=g^{j+1}(x),

\displaystyle F^{R_{m}}\circ\pi_{e,m}(x,j,k)=F^{R_{m}}\circ F^{k}\circ g^{j}(x)=g\circ g^{j}(x)=g^{j+1}(x),

where
F^{R_{m}}\circ F^{k}=g
follows from the fact that
F^{k}\circ g^{j}(x)
is already on the roof of
\Delta_{m}
. Therefore
\pi_{e,m}
is a semi-conjugacy.

Next we prove that
(\pi_{e,m})_{*}\mu_{\Delta_{e,m}}=\mu_{\Delta_{m}}
. Denote
\sum_{j}(F_{e}^{j})_{*}(\mu_{\Lambda}|_{R^{e}>j})
by
\nu
. Then for any
A\times\{k\}\subseteq\Delta_{m}
, where
A\subseteq\{R=i\}
for some
i\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(A\times\{k\})

\displaystyle=(\int\min\{R,m+1\}d\mu_{X})^{-1}\mu_{X}(A)

\displaystyle=(\int\min\{R,m+1\}d\mu_{X})^{-1}\mu_{\Delta_{e}}(\pi^{-1}_{e}A)

\displaystyle=(\int\min\{R,m+1\}d\mu_{X})^{-1}\mu_{\Delta_{e}}\{(x,n)\in\Lambda\times\{0,1,2,\cdots\}:g^{n}(x)\in A\}

\displaystyle=(\int\min\{R,m+1\}d\mu_{X})^{-1}(\int R^{e}d\mu_{\Lambda})^{-1}\nu(\pi_{e}^{-1}A).

On the other hand,

\displaystyle(\pi_{e,m})_{*}\mu_{\Delta_{e,m}}(A\times\{k\})

\displaystyle=\mu_{\Delta_{e,m}}\{\pi^{-1}_{e,m}(A\times\{k\})\}

\displaystyle=\mu_{\Delta_{e,m}}\{(x,j,i)\in\Delta_{e,m}:F^{i}g^{j}(x)\in A\times\{k\}\}

\displaystyle=\mu_{\Delta_{e,m}}\{(x,j,k)\in\Delta_{e,m}:g^{j}(x)\in A\}=(\int R_{e,m}d\mu_{\Lambda})^{-1}\nu(\pi_{e}^{-1}A).

Since
m_{i,j}=\min\{m,R\big(g^{j}(x)\big)-1\}
for
0\leq j\leq R^{e}_{i}-1,x\in\Lambda_{i}
, we have

\displaystyle\int\min\{R,m+1\}d\mu_{X}

\displaystyle=(\int R^{e}d\mu_{\Lambda})^{-1}\int\min\{R,m+1\}\circ\pi_{e}d\nu

\displaystyle=(\int R^{e}d\mu_{\Lambda})^{-1}\sum_{i\geq 1}\sum_{j<R^{e}_{i}}\min\{R,m+1\}|_{g^{j}\Lambda_{i}}\mu_{\Lambda}(\Lambda_{i})

\displaystyle=(\int R^{e}d\mu_{\Lambda})^{-1}\sum_{i\geq 1}\sum_{j<R^{e}_{i}}(1+m_{i,j})\mu_{\Lambda}(\Lambda_{i})

\displaystyle=(\int R^{e}d\mu_{\Lambda})^{-1}\int R_{e,m}d\mu_{\Lambda}.

Then

(\int\min\{R,m+1\}d\mu_{X})^{-1}(\int R^{e}d\mu_{\Lambda})^{-1}=(\int R_{e,m}d\mu_{\Lambda})^{-1}.

Therefore,
(\pi_{e,m})_{*}\mu_{\Delta_{e,m}}(A\times\{k\})=\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(A\times\{k\})
for any measurable set
A\times\{k\}\subseteq\Delta_{m}
. Since such set generates the
\sigma
-algebra of
\Delta_{m}
, then
(\pi_{e,m})_{*}\mu_{\Delta_{e,m}}=\mu_{\Delta_{m}}
.
∎

5.3
Thicker expanding quotient Young tower for
(\Delta_{m},F^{R_{m}},\mu_{\Delta_{m}})

Introduce an equivalence relation
\sim
on
\Delta_{e,m}
as

(x,j,k)\sim(y,i,l)\text{ if and only if }x,y\in\gamma^{s}\text{ for some }\gamma^{s}\in\Gamma^{s},j=i,k=l.

Using this equivalence relation, define a quotient tower
\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}}:=\Delta_{e,m}/\sim
, with canonical projections
\widetilde{\pi_{\Delta_{e,m}}}:\Delta_{e,m}\to\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}}
. We identify
\widetilde{\Lambda}
with
\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}}\bigcap(\widetilde{\Lambda}\times\{0\}\times\{0\})
. A quotient map
\widetilde{F_{e,m}}:\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}}\to\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}}
, and a quotient return time
\widetilde{R_{e,m}}:\widetilde{\Lambda}\to\mathbb{N}
are defined via the following relations

\displaystyle\widetilde{\pi_{\Delta_{e,m}}}\circ F_{e,m}=\widetilde{F_{e,m}}\circ\widetilde{\pi_{\Delta_{e,m}}},\quad R_{e,m}=\widetilde{R_{e,m}}\circ\widetilde{\pi_{\Lambda}}.

(5.8)

They satisfy to
\widetilde{g^{R^{e}}}=\widetilde{F_{e,m}}^{\widetilde{R_{e,m}}}
, which is easy to prove from the construction of
\Delta_{e,m}
and
F_{e,m}
.

Define a probability measure
\mu_{\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}}}
on
\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}}
as

\displaystyle\mu_{\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}}}:=(\int\widetilde{R_{e,m}}d\mu_{\widetilde{\Lambda}})^{-1}\sum_{j}(\widetilde{F_{e,m}}^{j})_{*}\big(\mu_{\widetilde{\Lambda}}|_{\{\widetilde{R_{e,m}}>j\}}\big).

Since
\widetilde{g^{R^{e}}}=\widetilde{F_{e}}^{\widetilde{R^{e}}}
, it is easy to see that
(\widetilde{\pi_{\Delta_{e,m}}})_{*}\mu_{\Delta_{e,m}}=\mu_{\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}}}
. By (
5.2
) we obtain the expression for distortions that for any
x,y\in\widetilde{\Lambda}
with
\widetilde{s}(x,y)\geq 1
,,

\displaystyle\log\frac{\det D\widetilde{F_{e,m}}^{\widetilde{R_{e,m}}}(x)}{\det D\widetilde{F_{e,m}}^{\widetilde{R_{e,m}}}(y)}\leq C\beta^{\widetilde{s}(\widetilde{F_{e,m}}^{\widetilde{R_{e,m}}}x,\widetilde{F_{e,m}}^{\widetilde{R_{e,m}}}y)},

(5.9)

where
\det D\widetilde{F_{e,m}}^{\widetilde{R_{e,m}}}
is the Radon-Nikodym derivative of
\widetilde{F_{e,m}}^{\widetilde{R_{e,m}}}
with respect to the measure
m
on
\widetilde{\Lambda}
.

Therefore
(\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}},\widetilde{F_{e,m}})
, together with (
5.9
), is the thicker expanding quotient Young tower for
(\Delta_{m},F^{R_{m}},\mu_{\Delta_{m}})
.

5.4
Decay of correlations

Suppose that
\gcd{\{R_{e,m}\}}=N_{e,m}
. Now we can define new towers

\displaystyle\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}:=\{(x,j,k)\in\Delta_{e,m}:x\in\Lambda_{i},k+\sum_{0\leq l<j}(1+m_{i,l})\equiv 0\pmod{N_{e,m}}\},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}}:=\{(x,j,k)\in\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}}:x\in\Lambda_{i},k+\sum_{0\leq l<j}(1+m_{i,l})\equiv 0\pmod{N_{e,m}}\},

which are sub-towers of
\Delta_{e,m}
and
\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}}
, respectively. Then the dynamics
F_{e,m}^{N_{e,m}}:\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}\to\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{F_{e,m}}^{N_{e,m}}:\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}}\to\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}}
preserve probability measures

\displaystyle\mu_{\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}}:=(\int R_{e,m}/N_{e,m}d\mu_{\Lambda})^{-1}\sum_{j}(F_{e,m}^{jN_{e,m}})_{*}\big(\mu_{\Lambda}|_{\{R_{e,m}/N_{e,m}>j\}}\big),

\displaystyle\mu_{\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}}}:=(\int\widetilde{R_{e,m}}/N_{e,m}d\mu_{\widetilde{\Lambda}})^{-1}\sum_{j}(\widetilde{F_{e,m}}^{jN_{e,m}})_{*}\big(\mu_{\widetilde{\Lambda}}|_{\{\widetilde{R_{e,m}}/N_{e,m}>j\}}\big),

respectively, and are mixing. Clearly,
(\widetilde{\pi_{\Delta_{e,m}}})_{*}\mu_{\Delta^{\prime}_{e,m}}=\mu_{\widetilde{\Delta^{\prime}_{e,m}}}
. Since
(\Delta_{m},F^{R_{m}},\mu_{\Delta_{m}})
is mixing (see Lemma
8
), then, by using the same argument as that on page 607 of
Young 1998
, we have

\displaystyle(\pi_{e,m})_{*}\mu_{\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}}=\mu_{\Delta_{m}}.

The following diagrams summarize all towers discussed so far.

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{e,m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{e,m}}
inclusion
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{e,m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{e,m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{e,m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{e,m}^{R_{e,m}}}
inclusion
inclusion
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F^{R_{m}})^{N_{e,m}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{R_{m}}}
inclusion
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

Now the families of partitions
(\mathcal{Q}^{m}_{k})_{k\geq 0}
of
\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}
, and
(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}^{m}_{k}})_{k\geq 0}
of
\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}}
are defined as

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}^{m}_{0}:=\{\Lambda_{i}\times\{j\}\times\{l\}\subseteq\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}:i\geq 1,j\geq 0,l\geq 0\},\quad\mathcal{Q}^{m}_{k}:=\bigvee_{0\leq i\leq k}(F_{e,m}^{N_{e,m}})^{-i}\mathcal{Q}^{m}_{0},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}^{m}_{0}}:=\{\widetilde{\Lambda_{i}}\times\{j\}\times\{l\}\subseteq\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}}:i\geq 1,j\geq 0,l\geq 0\},\quad\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}^{m}_{k}}:=\bigvee_{0\leq i\leq k}(\widetilde{F_{e,m}}^{N_{e,m}})^{-i}\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}^{m}_{0}}.

Then we have the following results.

Lemma 12

There is a constant
C_{\alpha}>0
, such that
\diam\left(\pi\circ\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}^{N_{e,m}k}(Q)\right)\leq C_{\alpha}\beta^{\frac{\alpha N_{e,m}k}{2m}}
and
\widetilde{\pi_{\Delta_{e,m}}}Q\in\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}^{m}_{2k}}
for any
Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{m}_{2k}
and any
k>m+1
. Moreover, there are constants
\beta_{m}\in(0,1)
and
C_{m}>0
, such that for any
n>2k\geq 2
, any
(\widetilde{Q_{i}})_{i\geq 1}\subseteq\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}^{m}_{k}}
, and any bounded function
\widetilde{h}:\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{R}
, we have the following estimate for decay of correlations

\Big|\int\mathbbm{1}_{\bigcup_{i\geq 1}\widetilde{Q_{i}}}\widetilde{h}\circ(\widetilde{F_{e,m}}^{N_{e,m}})^{n}d\mu_{\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}}}-\mu_{\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}}}\Big(\bigcup_{i\geq 1}\widetilde{Q_{i}}\Big)\int\widetilde{h}d\mu_{\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}}}\Big|\leq C_{m}\beta_{m}^{n-2k}\mu_{\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}}}\Big(\bigcup_{i\geq 1}\widetilde{Q_{i}}\Big)||\widetilde{h}||_{\infty}.

(5.10)

Besides, we also have the following estimate for decay of correlations. For any
n>2k\geq 2
, any
(Q_{i})_{i\geq 1}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{m}_{k}
, and for any
\sigma(\bigcup_{k\geq 0}\mathcal{Q}^{m}_{k})
-measurable function
h:\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle\Big|\int\mathbbm{1}_{\bigcup_{i\geq 1}Q_{i}}h\circ(F_{e,m}^{N_{e,m}})^{n}d\mu_{\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}}-\mu_{\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}}\Big(\bigcup_{i\geq 1}Q_{i}\Big)\int hd\mu_{\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}}\Big|\leq C_{m}\beta_{m}^{n-2k}\mu_{\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}}\Big(\bigcup_{i\geq 1}Q_{i}\Big)||h||_{\infty}.

(5.11)

Proof.

Since the return time for
\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}}
is
\widetilde{R_{e,m}}/N_{e,m}
, we have
\gcd\{\widetilde{R_{e,m}}/N_{e,m}\}=1
. The return map for
\widetilde{F_{e,m}}^{N_{e,m}}:\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}}\to\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}}
is
\big(\widetilde{F_{e,m}}^{N_{e,m}}\big)^{\widetilde{R_{e,m}}/N_{e,m}}:\widetilde{\Lambda}\to\widetilde{\Lambda}
, and it has the distortion:

\displaystyle\log\frac{\det D\big(\widetilde{F_{e,m}}^{N_{e,m}}\big)^{\widetilde{R_{e,m}}/N_{e,m}}(x)}{\det D\big(\widetilde{F_{e,m}}^{N_{e,m}}\big)^{\widetilde{R_{e,m}}/N_{e,m}}(y)}\leq C\beta^{\widetilde{s}\Big(\big(\widetilde{F_{e,m}}^{N_{e,m}}\big)^{\widetilde{R_{e,m}}/N_{e,m}}(x),\big(\widetilde{F_{e,m}}^{N_{e,m}}\big)^{\widetilde{R_{e,m}}/N_{e,m}}(y)\Big)}.

Therefore,
(\widetilde{\Delta_{e,m}^{\prime}},\widetilde{F_{e,m}}^{N_{e,m}})
is a mixing expanding Young tower. Besides, using (
5.8
), (
5.4
) and (
5.3
), we have

\displaystyle m\{x\in\widetilde{\Lambda}:\widetilde{R_{e,m}}(x)/N_{e,m}>n\}

\displaystyle\leq C\mu_{\widetilde{\Lambda}}\{x\in\widetilde{\Lambda}:\widetilde{R_{e,m}}(x)/N_{e,m}>n\}

\displaystyle=C\left(\widetilde{\pi_{\Lambda}}\right)_{*}\mu_{\Lambda}\{x\in\widetilde{\Lambda}:\widetilde{R_{e,m}}(x)/N_{e,m}>n\}

\displaystyle=C\mu_{\Lambda}\{x\in\Lambda:\widetilde{R_{e,m}}\circ\widetilde{\pi_{\Lambda}}(x)/N_{e,m}>n\}

\displaystyle=C\mu_{\Lambda}\{x\in\Lambda:R_{e,m}(x)/N_{e,m}>n\}

\displaystyle=C\int\mu_{\gamma^{u}}\{x\in\gamma^{u}:R_{e,m}(x)/N_{e,m}>n\}d\mu_{\Lambda}

\displaystyle\leq C^{2}\int\Leb_{\gamma^{u}}\{x\in\gamma^{u}:R_{e,m}(x)/N_{e,m}>n\}d\mu_{\Lambda}\leq C^{3}\big(\beta^{\frac{N_{e,m}}{2m}}\big)^{n},

where we applied Lemma
10
to the last inequality. By making use of Theorem 2 in
Young 1999
we get (
5.10
). Then (
5.11
)
follows from (
5.10
), (
5.8
) and
\left(\widetilde{\pi_{\Delta_{e,m}}}\right)_{*}\mu_{\Delta^{\prime}_{e,m}}=\mu_{\widetilde{\Delta^{\prime}_{e,m}}}
.

Next we estimate
\diam\left(\pi\circ\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}^{N_{e,m}k}(Q)\right)
. For any
\hat{\gamma}^{s}\subseteq Q
(here
\hat{\gamma}^{s}=\gamma^{s}\times\{l^{\prime}\}\times\{l\}
for some
\gamma^{s}\subseteq\Lambda
and
l^{\prime},l\geq 0
), assume that
\pi_{\mathbb{N}}\big(\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}^{N_{e,m}k}(\hat{\gamma}^{s})\big)=j\leq m
,
j^{\prime}\in[0,m]
is the first non-negative number such that
\pi_{e,m}\circ F^{j^{\prime}}_{e,m}(\hat{\gamma}^{s})\subseteq X
, and the disks
\pi\circ\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}^{j^{\prime}}(\hat{\gamma}^{s}),\pi\circ\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}^{j^{\prime}+1}(\hat{\gamma}^{s}),\cdots,\pi\circ\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}^{N_{e,m}k}(\hat{\gamma}^{s})
visit
X
exactly
q^{\prime}
times. Then

q^{\prime}+1\geq\frac{N_{e,m}k+1}{m+1}\geq N_{e,m}k/(2m),\quad g^{q^{\prime}-1}\circ\pi_{e,m}\circ F^{j^{\prime}}_{e,m}(\hat{\gamma}^{s})=\pi_{e,m}\circ F^{N_{e,m}k-j}_{e,m}(\hat{\gamma}^{s})\subseteq X

Then by Assumption
1
and by Definition
4
, there is a constant
C_{\alpha}>0
, such that

\displaystyle\diam\big(\pi\circ\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}^{N_{e,m}k}(\hat{\gamma}^{s})\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\diam\big(\pi\circ\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}^{N_{e,m}k-j}(\hat{\gamma}^{s})\big)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq C^{1+\alpha}\beta^{(q^{\prime}-1)\alpha}\diam\big(\pi\circ\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}^{j^{\prime}}(\hat{\gamma}^{s})\big)^{\alpha}\leq C_{\alpha}\beta^{\frac{N_{e,m}k\alpha}{2m}}/2.

On the other hand, for any
\hat{\gamma}^{u}\subseteq Q
(here
\hat{\gamma}^{u}=\gamma^{u}\times\{l^{\prime}\}\times\{l\}
for some
\gamma^{u}\subseteq\Lambda
and same
l^{\prime},l\geq 0
), suppose that
\pi_{\mathbb{N}}\big(\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}^{N_{e,m}k}(\hat{\gamma}^{u})\big)=i^{\prime}\leq m
,
\pi_{\mathbb{N}}\big(\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}^{N_{e,m}2k}(\hat{\gamma}^{u})\big)=i\leq m
, and the disks
\pi\circ\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}^{N_{e,m}k-i^{\prime}}(\hat{\gamma}^{u}),\pi\circ\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}^{N_{e,m}k-i^{\prime}+1}(\hat{\gamma}^{u}),\cdots,\pi\circ\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}^{N_{e,m}2k}(\hat{\gamma}^{u})
visit
X
exactly
q
times, then
q\geq\frac{N_{e,m}k+i^{\prime}+1}{m+1}\geq N_{e,m}k/(2m)
and

\displaystyle\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}^{N_{e,m}k-i^{\prime}}(\hat{\gamma}^{u})\subseteq X,\quad\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}^{N_{e,m}k-i^{\prime}}(\hat{\gamma}^{u})=g^{-(q-1)}\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}^{N_{e,m}2k-i}(\hat{\gamma}^{u})\subseteq X.

By Assumption
1
and by Definition
4
, there is a constant
C_{\alpha}>0
such that

\displaystyle\diam\big(\pi\circ\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}^{N_{e,m}k}(\hat{\gamma}^{u})\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\diam\big(\pi\circ\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}^{N_{e,m}k-i^{\prime}}(\hat{\gamma}^{u})\big)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq C^{1+\alpha}\beta^{\alpha(q-1)}\diam\big(\pi\circ\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}^{2N_{e,m}k-i}(\hat{\gamma}^{u})\big)^{\alpha}\leq C_{\alpha}\beta^{\frac{N_{e,m}k\alpha}{2m}}/2.

Finally, since any
\hat{\gamma}^{u},\hat{\gamma}^{s}\subseteq Q
intersect exactly at one point, then for any
x,y\in Q
there are
o\in\Lambda
and
\hat{\gamma}^{u}=\gamma^{u}(o)\times\{l^{\prime}\}\times\{l\}\subseteq Q,\hat{\gamma}^{s}=\gamma^{u}(o)\times\{l^{\prime}\}\times\{l\}\subseteq Q
, such that
x\in\hat{\gamma}^{u}
,
y\in\hat{\gamma}^{s}
, and

\displaystyle d\Big(\pi

\displaystyle\circ\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}^{N_{e,m}k}(x),\pi\circ\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}^{N_{e,m}k}(y)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\diam\Big(\pi\circ\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}^{N_{e,m}k}\big(\hat{\gamma}^{u}\big)\Big)+\diam\Big(\pi\circ\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}^{N_{e,m}k}\big(\hat{\gamma}^{s}\big)\Big)\leq C_{\alpha}\beta^{\frac{N_{e,m}k\alpha}{2m}}.

Therefore,
\diam\left(\pi\circ\pi_{e,m}\circ F_{e,m}^{N_{e,m}k}(Q)\right)\leq C_{\alpha}\beta^{\frac{N_{e,m}k\alpha}{2m}}
.∎

6
Poisson limit laws for non-mixing hyperbolic Young towers

Now we are ready to present sufficient conditions for (
4.9
). The approach for Poisson approximations, developed in
Bunimovich and Su 2022
,
Pène and Saussol 2020
,
Pène and Saussol 2016
, works for mixing hyperbolic Young towers. However, from the previous section, we know that our Young tower for
(\Delta_{e,m},F_{e,m},\mu_{\Delta_{e,m}})
is generally non-mixing. In this section we will prove Poisson limit laws for the dynamics
F^{R_{m}}
, which can be described by the non-mixing hyperbolic Young tower
(\Delta_{e,m},F_{e,m},\mu_{\Delta_{e,m}})
.

For any
i\geq 0
, we let

\displaystyle X_{i}:=\mathbbm{1}_{\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r}}\circ(F^{R_{m}})^{i},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{X}_{i}:=\Big(\mathbbm{1}_{\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r}}\circ(F^{R_{m}})^{N_{e,m}i},\mathbbm{1}_{\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r}}\circ(F^{R_{m}})^{N_{e,m}i+1},\cdots,\mathbbm{1}_{\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r}}\circ(F^{R_{m}})^{N_{e,m}(i+1)-1}\Big).

Observe that
(\boldsymbol{X}_{i})_{i\geq 0}
is stationary, since
\mu_{\Delta_{m}}
is
(F^{R_{m}})^{N_{e,m}}
-invariant. Denote by
\{\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i}\}_{i\geq 0}
i.i.d. random vectors defined on a probability space
(\hat{\Omega},\hat{\mathbb{P}})
, such that for each
i\geq 0

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{X}_{i}=_{d}\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i},

i.e., they have the same distribution. Throughout this section the notation
h(\underbrace{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}},\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}},\cdots,\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}}}_{\text{k}})
means that a function
h
is defined on
\mathbb{R}^{k}
for some
k\geq 1
, and
h\in[0,1]
means that a function
h
takes values in
[0,1]
. Further,
\mathbb{E}
is the expectation of
\mu_{\Delta_{m}}\otimes\hat{\mathbb{P}}
. Denote by
\boldsymbol{0}
the zero vector in
\{0,1\}^{N_{e,m}}
. For any vector
\boldsymbol{a}\in\{0,1\}^{N_{e,m}}
,
\boldsymbol{a}\geq 1
means that at least one of the coordinates of
\boldsymbol{a}
is not zero, and
\boldsymbol{a}=\boldsymbol{0}
means that all coordinates of
\boldsymbol{a}
are zero.

The scheme of our proof can be roughly described as follows. In order to give sufficient conditions for (
4.9
), we will approximate
(X_{i})_{i\geq 0}
by i.i.d. random variables in Lemma
15
. To achieve this, we first approximate
(X_{i})_{i\geq 0}
by i.i.d. random vectors
(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i})_{i\geq 0}
in Lemma
13
, then approximate the i.i.d. random vectors
(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i})_{i\geq 0}
by i.i.d. random variables in Lemma
14
. Now we turn to the proofs.

Lemma 13

For any
n\geq 1
and any integer
p\in(0,n)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[h(\boldsymbol{X}_{0},\boldsymbol{X}_{1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})-h(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{0},\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{1},\cdots,\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{n})\Big]\Big|\precsim_{m}R_{1}+R_{2}+R_{3},

where

\displaystyle R_{1}:=\sum_{0\leq l\leq n-p}\sup_{\boldsymbol{a}\geq 1}\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{0}=\boldsymbol{a}}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n-l})\Big]-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{0}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n-l})\Big|

\displaystyle R_{2}:=\sup_{\boldsymbol{a}\geq 1}n\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{0}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{1\leq j\leq p-1}\boldsymbol{X}_{j}\geq 1}\right)

\displaystyle R_{3}:=pn\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})^{2}+p\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r}),

and a constant in
``\precsim_{m}"
depends only on
m
.

Proof.

Now we can estimate

\displaystyle\sup_{h\in[0,1]}

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[h(\boldsymbol{X}_{0},\boldsymbol{X}_{1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})-h(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{0},\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{1},\cdots,\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{n})\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle=\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\sum_{0\leq l\leq n}\mathbb{E}h\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{0},\cdots,\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{l-1},\boldsymbol{X}_{l},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n}\right)-\mathbb{E}h\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{0},\cdots,\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{l-1},\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{l},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n}\right)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\sum_{0\leq l\leq n}\mathbb{E}h_{l}\Big(\boldsymbol{X}_{l},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n}\Big)-\mathbb{E}h_{l}\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{l},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n}\right)\Big|,

where
h_{l}(\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}}):=h(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{0},\cdots,\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{l-1},\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}})
. Since
\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{0},\cdots,\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{l-1}
are independent of other random variables, then, without loss of generality, we can assume that the function
h_{l}
does not depend on
\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{0},\cdots,\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{l-1}
. Note that
\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=_{d}\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{l}
are
\{0,1\}^{N_{e,m}}
-valued random vectors. Thus

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}h_{l}\Big(\boldsymbol{X}_{l},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n}\Big)-\mathbb{E}h_{l}\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{l},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n}\right)\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{0}}h_{l}(\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big]+\sum_{\boldsymbol{a}\geq 1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}h_{l}(\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big]

\displaystyle\quad-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{l}=\boldsymbol{0}}\mathbb{E}h_{l}(\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})-\sum_{\boldsymbol{a}\geq 1}\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}h_{l}(\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\sum_{\boldsymbol{a}\geq 1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}h_{l}(\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big]+\sum_{\boldsymbol{a}\geq 1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}h_{l}(\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big]

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{\boldsymbol{a}\geq 1}\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}h_{l}(\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})-\sum_{\boldsymbol{a}\geq 1}\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}h_{l}(\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{\boldsymbol{a}\geq 1}\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big]-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big|.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sup_{h\in[0,1]}

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[h(\boldsymbol{X}_{0},\boldsymbol{X}_{1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})-h(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{0},\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{1},\cdots,\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{n})\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{0\leq l\leq n}\sum_{\boldsymbol{a}\geq 1}\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big]-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big|.

(6.1)

We start with estimating the terms with
l\leq n-p
in (
6.1
).

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big]-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big]-\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}h(\boldsymbol{0},\cdots,\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}h(\boldsymbol{0},\cdots,\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big]-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}h(\boldsymbol{0},\cdots,\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}h(\boldsymbol{0},\cdots,\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big\{\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}\Big[h(\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})-h(\boldsymbol{0},\cdots,\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big]\Big\}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}\Big[h(\boldsymbol{0},\cdots,\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})-h(\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}h(\boldsymbol{0},\cdots,\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big]-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}h(\boldsymbol{0},\cdots,\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big|.

Observe that

|h(\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})-h(\boldsymbol{0},\cdots,\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})|\leq 2\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{l+1\leq j\leq l+p-1}\boldsymbol{X}_{j}\geq 1}.

Now, in view of stationarity of
(\boldsymbol{X}_{i})_{i\geq 0}
, we can continue estimates as

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}h(\boldsymbol{0},\cdots,\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big]-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}h\left(\boldsymbol{0},\cdots,\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n}\right)\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+2\mathbb{E}\Big(\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{l+1\leq j\leq l+p-1}\boldsymbol{X}_{j}\geq 1}\Big)+2\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{l+1\leq j\leq l+p-1}\boldsymbol{X}_{j}\geq 1}

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}h(\boldsymbol{0},\cdots,\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big]-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}h(\boldsymbol{0},\cdots,\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+2\mathbb{E}\Big(\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{0}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{1\leq j\leq p-1}\boldsymbol{X}_{j}\geq 1}\Big)+2\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{0}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{1\leq j\leq p-1}\boldsymbol{X}_{j}\geq 1}.

Observe that

\displaystyle\{\sum_{1\leq j\leq p-1}\boldsymbol{X}_{j}\geq 1\}=\bigcup_{N_{e,m}\leq j\leq N_{e,m}p-1}(F^{R_{m}})^{-j}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r}),\quad\boldsymbol{a}\geq 1,

\displaystyle\{\boldsymbol{X}_{0}=\boldsymbol{a}\}\subseteq\bigcup_{0\leq j\leq N_{e,m}-1}(F^{R_{m}})^{-j}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r}).

Hence, we can continue the sequence of inequalities above as

\displaystyle\precsim_{m}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}h(\boldsymbol{0},\cdots,\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big]-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}h(\boldsymbol{0},\cdots,\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{0}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{1\leq j\leq p-1}\boldsymbol{X}_{j}\geq 1}\right)+p\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})^{2}.

Therefore for the terms with
l\leq n-p
in (
6.1
) we have

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big]-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big|

\displaystyle\precsim_{m}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}h(\boldsymbol{0},\cdots,\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big]-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}h(\boldsymbol{0},\cdots,\boldsymbol{0},\boldsymbol{X}_{l+p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{0}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{1\leq j\leq p-1}\boldsymbol{X}_{j}\geq 1}\right)+p\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})^{2}.

Consider now the terms with
l>n-p
in (
6.1
). Since
\boldsymbol{a}\geq 1
and

\displaystyle\{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}\}\subseteq\bigcup_{lN_{e,m}\leq j\leq(l+1)N_{e,m}-1}(F^{R_{m}})^{-j}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r}),\quad||h||_{\infty}\leq 1,

then

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big]-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big|\precsim_{m}\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r}).

Therefore

\displaystyle(\ref{sum})

\displaystyle=2\sum_{0\leq l\leq n}\sup_{\boldsymbol{a}\geq 1}\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big]-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big|

\displaystyle\precsim_{m}\sum_{0\leq l\leq n-p}\sup_{\boldsymbol{a}\geq 1}\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{l+p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big]-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{l}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{l+p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\sup_{\boldsymbol{a}\geq 1}n\mathbb{E}\Big(\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{0}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{1\leq j\leq p-1}\boldsymbol{X}_{j}\geq 1}\Big)+pn\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})^{2}+p\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r}).

By making use of stationarity of
(\boldsymbol{X}_{i})_{i\geq 0}
, the last expression above can be estimated as

\displaystyle\precsim_{m}\sum_{0\leq l\leq n-p}\sup_{\boldsymbol{a}\geq 1}\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{0}=\boldsymbol{a}}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n-l})\Big]-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{0}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n-l})\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\sup_{\boldsymbol{a}\geq 1}n\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{0}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{1\leq j\leq p-1}\boldsymbol{X}_{j}\geq 1}\right)+pn\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})^{2}+p\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r}),

which concludes a proof.
∎

Denote by
\{\hat{X}_{i}\}_{i\geq 0}
i.i.d. random variables, which do not depend on
(X_{i})_{i\geq 0}
and
(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i})_{i\geq 0}
, and which are defined on a probability space
(\hat{\Omega},\hat{\mathbb{P}})
such that for each
i\geq 0
,

X_{i}=_{d}\hat{X}_{i}.

Define now random vectors

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{Y}_{i}:=(\hat{X}_{iN_{e,m}},\hat{X}_{iN_{e,m}+1},\cdots,\hat{X}_{(i+1)N_{e,m}-1}).

As the next step we will approximate
(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i})_{i\geq 0}
by
(\boldsymbol{Y}_{i})_{i\geq 0}
.

Lemma 14

For any
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[h(\boldsymbol{Y}_{0},\boldsymbol{Y}_{1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{Y}_{n})-h(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{0},\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{1},\cdots,\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{n})\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle\precsim_{m}n\mathbb{E}\big(\mathbbm{1}_{X_{0}=1}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{1\leq j\leq N_{e,m}-1}X_{j}\geq 1}\big)+n\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})^{2},

where a constant in
``\precsim_{m}"
depends only on
m
.

Proof.

Assume that
\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{0}:=(Z_{0},Z_{1},\cdots,Z_{N_{e,m}-1})
. Note that all
Z_{i}
are not independent, and for all
0\leq i\leq N_{e,m}-1
, we have

\displaystyle Z_{i}=_{d}X_{i}=_{d}\hat{X}_{i}.

We can start now the next estimate.

\displaystyle\sup_{h\in[0,1]}

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[h(\boldsymbol{Y}_{0},\boldsymbol{Y}_{1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{Y}_{n})-h(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{0},\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{1},\cdots,\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{n})\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle=\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\sum_{0\leq l\leq n}\mathbb{E}h\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{0},\cdots,\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{l-1},\boldsymbol{Y}_{l},\cdots,\boldsymbol{Y}_{n}\right)-\mathbb{E}h\left(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{0},\cdots,\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{l-1},\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{l},\boldsymbol{Y}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{Y}_{n}\right)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\sum_{0\leq l\leq n}\mathbb{E}h^{\prime}_{l}(\boldsymbol{Y}_{l})-\mathbb{E}h^{\prime}_{l}(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{l})\Big|\leq\sum_{0\leq l\leq n}\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}h^{\prime}_{l}(\boldsymbol{Y}_{l})-\mathbb{E}h^{\prime}_{l}(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{l})\Big|,

where
h^{\prime}_{l}(\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}}):=h(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{1},\cdots,\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{l-1},\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}},\boldsymbol{Y}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{Y}_{n})
. Since
\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{1},\cdots,\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{l-1},\boldsymbol{Y}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{Y}_{n}
do not depend on
\boldsymbol{Y}_{l}
and
\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{l}
. Then, without a loss of generality,
h^{\prime}_{l}
can be viewed as a function which does not depend on
\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{1},\cdots,\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{l-1},\boldsymbol{Y}_{l+1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{Y}_{n}
. By stationarity of
(\boldsymbol{Y}_{i})_{i\geq 0}
and
(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i})_{i\geq 0}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}h^{\prime}_{l}(\boldsymbol{Y}_{l})

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}h^{\prime}_{l}(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{l})\Big|\leq\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}h(\boldsymbol{Y}_{l})-\mathbb{E}h(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{l})\Big|=\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}h(\boldsymbol{Y}_{0})-\mathbb{E}h(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{0})\Big|

\displaystyle=\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\sum_{0\leq l\leq N_{e,m}-1}\mathbb{E}h\left(\hat{X}_{0},\cdots,\hat{X}_{l-1},Z_{l},\cdots,Z_{N_{e,m}-1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\mathbb{E}h\left(\hat{X}_{0},\cdots,\hat{X}_{l-1},\hat{X}_{l},Z_{l+1},\cdots,Z_{N_{e,m}-1}\right)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\sum_{0\leq l\leq N_{e,m}-1}\mathbb{E}h_{l}(Z_{l},Z_{l+1},\cdots,Z_{N_{e,m}-1})-\mathbb{E}h_{l}(\hat{X}_{l},Z_{l+1},\cdots,Z_{N_{e,m}-1})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{0\leq l\leq N_{e,m}-1}\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}h_{l}(Z_{l},Z_{l+1},\cdots,Z_{N_{e,m}-1})-\mathbb{E}h_{l}(\hat{X}_{l},Z_{l+1},\cdots,Z_{N_{e,m}-1})\Big|,

where
h_{l}(\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}}):=h(\hat{X}_{1},\cdots,\hat{X}_{l-1},\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{$\bullet$}}})
. As before,
h_{l}
can be regarded as a function which does not depend on
\hat{X}_{1},\cdots,\hat{X}_{l-1}
. Note that
X_{l}=_{d}\hat{X}_{l}=_{d}Z_{l}
are
\{0,1\}
-valued random variables. Thus

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}h_{l}\Big(Z_{l},Z_{l+1},\cdots,Z_{N_{e,m}-1}\Big)-\mathbb{E}h_{l}\left(\hat{X}_{l},Z_{l+1},\cdots,Z_{N_{e,m}-1}\right)\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{Z_{l}=0}h_{l}(0,Z_{l+1},\cdots,Z_{N_{e,m}-1})\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{Z_{l}=1}h_{l}(1,Z_{l+1},\cdots,Z_{N_{e,m}-1})\Big]

\displaystyle\quad-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\hat{X}_{l}=0}\mathbb{E}h_{l}(0,Z_{l+1},\cdots,Z_{N_{e,m}-1})-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\hat{X}_{l}=1}\mathbb{E}h_{l}(1,Z_{l+1},\cdots,Z_{N_{e,m}-1})\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{Z_{l}=1}h_{l}(0,Z_{l+1},\cdots,Z_{N_{e,m}-1})\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{Z_{l}=1}h_{l}(1,Z_{l+1},\cdots,Z_{N_{e,m}-1})\Big]

\displaystyle\quad-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\hat{X}_{l}=1}\mathbb{E}h_{l}(0,Z_{l+1},\cdots,Z_{N_{e,m}-1})-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\hat{X}_{l}=1}\mathbb{E}h_{l}(1,Z_{l+1},\cdots,Z_{N_{e,m}-1})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{Z_{l}=1}h(Z_{l+1},\cdots,Z_{N_{e,m}-1})\Big]-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{Z_{l}=1}\mathbb{E}h(Z_{l+1},\cdots,Z_{N_{e,m}-1})\Big|

\displaystyle=2\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{Z_{l}=1}h(Z_{l+1},\cdots,Z_{N_{e,m}-1})-\mathbbm{1}_{Z_{l}=1}h(0,\cdots,0)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{Z_{l}=1}\mathbb{E}\Big[h(Z_{l+1},\cdots,Z_{N_{e,m}-1})-h(0,\cdots,0)\Big]\Big|.

Using that
|h(Z_{l+1},\cdots,Z_{N_{e,m}-1})-h(0,\cdots,0)|\leq 2\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{l+1\leq j\leq N_{e,m}-1}Z_{j}\geq 1}
, stationarity of
(X_{i})_{i=0}^{N_{e,m}-1}
and of
(Z_{i})_{i=0}^{N_{e,m}-1}
, and the relation
(X_{0},X_{1},\cdots,X_{N_{e,m}-1})=_{d}(Z_{0},Z_{1},\cdots,Z_{N_{e,m}-1})
, we can continue the estimate above as

\displaystyle\precsim\mathbb{E}\big(\mathbbm{1}_{Z_{l}=1}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{l+1\leq j\leq N_{e,m}-1}Z_{j}\geq 1}\big)+\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{Z_{l}=1}\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{l+1\leq j\leq N_{e,m}-1}Z_{j}\geq 1}

\displaystyle\precsim\mathbb{E}\big(\mathbbm{1}_{Z_{0}=1}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{1\leq j\leq N_{e,m}-1}Z_{j}\geq 1}\big)+\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{Z_{0}=1}\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{1\leq j\leq N_{e,m}-1}Z_{j}\geq 1}

\displaystyle\precsim\mathbb{E}\big(\mathbbm{1}_{X_{0}=1}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{1\leq j\leq N_{e,m}-1}X_{j}\geq 1}\big)+\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{X_{0}=1}\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{1\leq j\leq N_{e,m}-1}X_{j}\geq 1}

\displaystyle\precsim\mathbb{E}\big(\mathbbm{1}_{X_{0}=1}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{1\leq j\leq N_{e,m}-1}X_{j}\geq 1}\big)+\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{1\leq j\leq N_{e,m}-1}X_{j}\geq 1}

\displaystyle\precsim\mathbb{E}\big(\mathbbm{1}_{X_{0}=1}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{1\leq j\leq N_{e,m}-1}X_{j}\geq 1}\big)+N_{e,m}\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})^{2},

where the last inequality is due to
\{\sum_{1\leq j\leq N_{e,m}-1}X_{j}\geq 1\}\subseteq\bigcup_{0\leq j\leq N_{e,m}-1}(F^{R_{m}})^{-j}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})
.

By combining all estimates above we get

\displaystyle\sup_{h\in[0,1]}

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[h(\boldsymbol{Y}_{0},\boldsymbol{Y}_{1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{Y}_{n})-h(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{0},\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{1},\cdots,\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{n})\Big]\Big|\leq\sum_{0\leq l\leq n}\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}h^{\prime}_{l}(\boldsymbol{Y}_{l})-\mathbb{E}h^{\prime}_{l}(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{l})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{0\leq l\leq n}\sum_{0\leq l^{\prime}\leq N_{e,m}-1}\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}h_{l^{\prime}}(Z_{l^{\prime}},\cdots,Z_{N_{e,m}-1})-\mathbb{E}h_{l^{\prime}}(\hat{X}_{l^{\prime}},Z_{l^{\prime}+1},\cdots,Z_{N_{e,m}-1})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{0\leq l\leq n}\sum_{0\leq l^{\prime}\leq N_{e,m}-1}\big[\mathbb{E}\big(\mathbbm{1}_{X_{0}=1}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{1\leq j\leq N_{e,m}-1}X_{j}\geq 1}\big)+N_{e,m}\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\precsim_{m}n\mathbb{E}\big(\mathbbm{1}_{X_{0}=1}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{1\leq j\leq N_{e,m}-1}X_{j}\geq 1}\big)+n\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})^{2},

which concludes a proof.
∎

By making use of Lemmas
13
and
14
, we can simplify now (
4.9
).

Lemma 15

For any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, and for any disjoint bounded intervals
J_{1},J_{2},\cdots,J_{k}\subseteq[0,\infty)
, let

\displaystyle n:=\max\{i:i\cdot\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})\in J_{j},j=1,2,\cdots,k\},\quad p:=n^{1-\epsilon}.

Then

\displaystyle\Big|\mu_{\Delta_{m}}\Big(\mathcal{N}_{i}^{r}(\bigcup_{j\leq k}J_{j})=0\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathcal{P}(\bigcup_{j\leq k}J_{j})=0\Big)\Big|\precsim_{m}T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3},

where

\displaystyle T_{1}:=\sum_{0\leq l\leq n-p}\sup_{\boldsymbol{a}\geq 1}\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{0}=\boldsymbol{a}}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n-l})\Big]-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{0}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n-l})\Big|

(6.2)

\displaystyle T_{2}:=\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{X_{0}=1}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{1\leq j\leq pN_{e,m}-1}X_{j}\geq 1}\right)

(6.3)

\displaystyle T_{3}:=\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})^{\epsilon},

where a constant in
``\precsim_{m}"
depends only on
m
. Since
T_{3}\precsim_{m}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(S_{r})^{\epsilon}\to 0
when
r\to 0
, then, in order to prove (
4.9
), we just need to show that (
6.2
) and (
6.3
) converge to zero as
r\to 0
.

We will say in what follows that (
6.3
) is a short return.

Proof.

Let
J_{i}^{\prime}:=\{j:j\cdot\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})\in J_{i}\}
,
X_{J_{i}^{\prime}}:=\sum_{j\in J_{i}^{\prime}}\mathbbm{1}_{\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r}}\circ(F^{R_{m}})^{j}
, and
\hat{X}_{J_{i}^{\prime}}:=\sum_{j\in J_{i}^{\prime}}\hat{X}_{j}
, where
\{\hat{X}_{i}\}_{i\geq 0}
are i.i.d. random variables such that
\hat{X}_{i}=_{d}X_{i}=\mathbbm{1}_{\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r}}\circ(F^{R_{m}})^{i}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\Big|\mu_{\Delta_{m}}\Big(\mathcal{N}_{i}^{r}(\bigcup_{j\leq k}J_{j})=0\Big)-\mathbb{P}\Big(

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}(\bigcup_{j\leq k}J_{j})=0\Big)\Big|\leq\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[h(X_{J_{1}^{\prime}},\cdots,X_{J_{k}^{\prime}})-h\Big(\mathcal{P}(J_{1}),\cdots,\mathcal{P}(J_{k})\Big)\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[h(X_{J_{1}^{\prime}},\cdots,X_{J_{k}^{\prime}})-h(\hat{X}_{J_{1}^{\prime}},\cdots,\hat{X}_{J_{k}^{\prime}})\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[h\Big(\mathcal{P}(J_{1}),\cdots,\mathcal{P}(J_{k})\Big)-h(\hat{X}_{J_{1}^{\prime}},\cdots,\hat{X}_{J_{k}^{\prime}})\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[h(X_{0},X_{1},\cdots,X_{n})-h(\hat{X}_{0},\hat{X}_{1},\cdots,\hat{X}_{n})\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[h\Big(\mathcal{P}(J_{1}),\cdots,\mathcal{P}(J_{k})\Big)-h(\hat{X}_{J_{1}^{\prime}},\cdots,\hat{X}_{J_{k}^{\prime}})\Big]\Big|.

(6.4)

By applying Theorem 2 from
Arratia et al. 1989
to
(\hat{X}_{i})_{i\geq 0}
we get

\displaystyle\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[h\Big(P(J_{1}),\cdots,P(J_{k})\Big)-h\left(\hat{X}_{J^{\prime}_{1}},\cdots,\hat{X}_{J^{\prime}_{k}}\right)\Big]\Big|\precsim n\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})^{2}.

Since
(X_{0},\cdots,X_{n})
,
(\hat{X}_{0},\hat{X}_{1},\cdots,\hat{X}_{n})
are some entries of
(\boldsymbol{X}_{0},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})
,
(\boldsymbol{Y}_{0},\boldsymbol{Y}_{1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{Y}_{n})
, respectively, then

\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[h(X_{0},\cdots,X_{n})-h(\hat{X}_{0},\cdots,\hat{X}_{n})\Big]\Big|\leq\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[h(\boldsymbol{X}_{0},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})-h(\boldsymbol{Y}_{0},\cdots,\boldsymbol{Y}_{n})\Big]\Big|.

Now we can continue the estimate (
6.4
) as

\displaystyle\precsim_{m}\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[h(X_{0},X_{1},\cdots,X_{n})-h(\hat{X}_{0},\hat{X}_{1},\cdots,\hat{X}_{n})\Big]\Big|+n\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})^{2}

\displaystyle\precsim_{m}\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[h(\boldsymbol{X}_{0},\boldsymbol{X}_{1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n})-h(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{0},\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{1},\cdots,\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{n})\Big]\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[h(\boldsymbol{Y}_{0},\boldsymbol{Y}_{1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{Y}_{n})-h(\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{0},\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{1},\cdots,\hat{\boldsymbol{X}}_{n})\Big]\Big|+n\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})^{2}

\displaystyle\precsim_{m}R_{1}+R_{2}+R_{3}+n\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{X_{0}=1}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{1\leq j\leq N_{e,m}-1}X_{j}\geq 1}+n\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})^{2},

where the last “
\precsim_{m}
” is due to Lemmas
13
and
14
.

Using
n\precsim\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})^{-1}
(where a constant in
``\precsim"
depends on
\max\{n^{\prime}:n^{\prime}\in J_{i},i=1,2,\cdots,k\}<\infty
),
p=n^{1-\epsilon}
and stationarity of
(X_{i})_{i\geq 0}
, we obtain

\displaystyle R_{2}=\sup_{\boldsymbol{a}\geq 1}n\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{0}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{1\leq j\leq p-1}\boldsymbol{X}_{j}\geq 1}\right)\precsim\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{X_{0}=1}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{1\leq j\leq pN_{e,m}-1}X_{j}\geq 1}\right),

\displaystyle n\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{X_{0}=1}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{1\leq j\leq N_{e,m}-1}X_{j}\geq 1}\precsim\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{X_{0}=1}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{1\leq j\leq pN_{e,m}-1}X_{j}\geq 1}\right).

Then we can continue the estimate above as

\displaystyle\precsim_{m}\sum_{0\leq l\leq n-p}\sup_{\boldsymbol{a}\geq 1}\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{0}=\boldsymbol{a}}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n-l})\Big]-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{0}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n-l})\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{X_{0}=1}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{1\leq j\leq pN_{e,m}-1}X_{j}\geq 1}\right)+\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})^{\epsilon},

which concludes a proof.
∎

7
Short returns

From Lemma
15
, we just need to prove (
6.2
) and (
6.3
). In this section we will estimate short returns (
6.3
). The papers
Pène and Saussol 2010
,
Pène and Saussol 2020
provided effective methods to estimate (
6.3
) for Sinai billiards with bounded horizons and for diamond billiards. Some specific properties (e.g. bounded free paths and complexity of singularities for these two billiards) were used there. In contrast, we are using here only the hyperbolic product structure
\Lambda
in hyperbolic Young towers.

First of all we will show that the short return (
6.3
) on
\Delta_{m}
can be reduced to the short return on
X
.

Lemma 16

(Reduce (
6.3
))

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{X_{0}=1}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{1\leq j\leq pN_{e,m}-1}X_{j}\geq 1}\right)\precsim_{m}\int\mathbbm{1}_{\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r}}\mathbbm{1}_{\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq pN_{e,m}}(f^{R})^{-j}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r})}d\mu_{X},

\displaystyle\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})\approx_{m}\mu_{X}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r}).

Proof.

Since
S_{r}
is a section, then for any
x\in\Delta_{m}
,

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r}}(x)\mathbbm{1}_{\bigcup_{j=1}^{pN_{e,m}-1}(F^{R_{m}})^{-j}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})}(x)\leq\mathbbm{1}_{\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r}}(\pi_{X}x)\mathbbm{1}_{\bigcup_{j=1}^{pN_{e,m}}(f^{R})^{-j}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r})}(\pi_{X}x),

\displaystyle(\pi_{X})_{*}(\mu_{\Delta_{m}}|_{\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r}})=(\int\min\{R,m+1\}d\mu_{X})^{-1}\mu_{X}|_{\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{X_{0}=1}\mathbbm{1}_{\sum_{1\leq j\leq pN_{e,m}-1}X_{j}\geq 1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r}}\mathbbm{1}_{\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r}}\circ\pi_{X}\mathbbm{1}_{\bigcup_{j=1}^{pN_{e,m}}(f^{R})^{-j}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r})}\circ\pi_{X}d\mu_{\Delta_{m}}

\displaystyle\precsim_{m}\int\mathbbm{1}_{\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r}}\mathbbm{1}_{\bigcup_{j=1}^{pN_{e,m}}(f^{R})^{-j}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r})}d\mu_{X}.

The relation
\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})\approx_{m}\mu_{X}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r})
holds since
S_{r}
is a section, which concludes a proof of the lemma.
∎

Therefore, in order to prove that (
6.3
) converges to zero, the relation

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to 0}\frac{1}{\mu_{X}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r})}\int\mathbbm{1}_{\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r}}\mathbbm{1}_{\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq pN_{e,m}-1}(f^{R})^{-j}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r})}d\mu_{X}=0

will be proved in Lemma
25
, accompanied by several technical lemmas in the following subsections. We will use for that decay of correlations in the dynamical system
(X,(f^{R})^{N_{e}},\mu_{X})
and mixing hyperbolic Young towers
(\Delta_{e}^{\prime},F_{e}^{N_{e}},\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}})
, where
N_{e}
is the one from Lemma
7
.

Throughout this section, in order to simplify notations, we let
g=f^{R}
, and still use
\pi_{e}
to denote the semi-conjugacy from
\Delta_{e}^{\prime}
to
X
, i.e.,
\pi_{e}:\Delta_{e}^{\prime}\to X
satisfies

\displaystyle g^{N_{e}}\circ\pi_{e}=\pi_{e}\circ F_{e}^{N_{e}}.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-41.0697pt}{-13.98254pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{12.24348pt}{17.92027pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{{{{{}}{

{}{}}{}{}{{}{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}{}{}{{{}{}}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}{{{}{}}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -57.01 -25.02 L -19.98 -25.02}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}{{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-14.24022pt}{-18.08531pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
