{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-34.84984pt}{-14.37146pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

This is possible since
(\pi_{e})_{*}\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}=\mu_{X}
by Lemma
7
. Besides, throughout this section, we define
\pi_{\partial}:\mathcal{M}=\partial Q\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\to\partial Q
by

\pi_{\partial}(q,v)=q,

and suppose that

\displaystyle R|_{X_{n}}=R_{n}^{\prime}\text{ for some }R_{n}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}.

(7.1)

It is the case because
X
is divided by a part of
\mathbb{S}
into countably many pieces
X=\bigcup_{i}X_{i}
, so that
R
is constant on each
X_{i}
(see Definition
2
).

7.1
Return statistics

In this subsection, we will prove that for a.e.
\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathcal{M}
,

\displaystyle\liminf_{r\to 0}\frac{\log\mathcal{Z}_{2r}(\boldsymbol{m})}{-\log\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})}\geq 1,

where for any
\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathcal{M}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{r}(\boldsymbol{m}):=\min\{n\geq 1:g^{n}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\boldsymbol{m})\in\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\big(B_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\big)\},

\displaystyle S_{r}\text{ is the section contained in the quasi-section }B_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2].

This inequality will be proved in Lemma
23
, followed by a series of technical lemmas in this subsection.

Define for any
n\geq 1,r>0
,

\displaystyle A_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m}):=\bigcup_{0\leq i\leq N_{e}-1}g^{i}\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\big(B_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\big)

\displaystyle G_{n,r}:=\{\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathcal{M}:(g^{N_{e}})^{n}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\boldsymbol{m})\in A_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\}.

Fix
p^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
, which will be exactly determined later. From Lemma
7
almost surely

\displaystyle\bigcup_{Z\in\mathcal{Q}_{2p^{\prime}}}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}Z=g^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}(\pi_{e}\bigcup_{Z\in\mathcal{Q}_{2p^{\prime}}}Z)=X=\bigcup_{i}X_{i}\text{ almost surely}.

For each
Z\in\mathcal{Q}_{2p^{\prime}}
, we know that
\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}Z
is the intersection of a family of stable disks and a family of unstable disks. Thus by Assumption
1

\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}Z
is completely contained in some
X_{i}
. Therefore for each
X_{i}
we define a family of sets
(Z_{ji})_{j}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}_{2p^{\prime}}
such that each
Z_{ji}
satisfies the relation

\displaystyle\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}Z_{ji}\subseteq X_{i}.

Therefore
\bigcup_{j}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}Z_{ji}\subseteq X_{i}
.
In fact we have a stronger result.

Lemma 17

\bigcup_{j}Z_{ji}=F_{e}^{-N_{e}p^{\prime}}\pi_{e}^{-1}X_{i}
almost surely, which implies that
\bigcup_{j}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}Z_{ji}=X_{i}
almost surely.

Proof.

For a.e.
x\in X_{i}
there is
z\in Z\subseteq\Delta_{e}
for some
Z\in\mathcal{Q}_{2p^{\prime}}
, such that
\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}z=x
. By Assumption
1
we know that
\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}Z
is completely contained in some
X_{j}
, which must be
X_{i}
, since
\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}Z
also contains
x\in X_{i}
. From the way how
Z_{ji}
was chosen, we know that
Z
is one of the
Z_{ji}
. Therefore
\bigcup_{j}Z_{ji}=F_{e}^{-N_{e}p^{\prime}}\pi_{e}^{-1}X_{i}
almost surely.
∎

Since
\mathcal{M}=\bigcup_{i}\bigcup_{k<R_{i}^{\prime}}f^{k}X_{i}
, in order to estimate
\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(G_{n,r})
, we will estimate a measure of
f^{k}X_{i}\bigcap G_{n,r}=f^{k}(\bigcup_{j}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}Z_{ji})\bigcap G_{n,r}
, where
k<R_{i}^{\prime}
. And finally we will sum up these estimates.

For each
Z_{ji}
, we choose and fix
m_{jik}\in f^{k}(\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}Z_{ji})\bigcap G_{n,r}
. Then the following statement holds.

Lemma 18

There is
C_{\alpha}>0
such that for each
k<R_{i}^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(f^{k}X_{i}\bigcap G_{n,r})\leq\sum_{j}\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}\Big(Z_{ji}\bigcap F_{e}^{-N_{e}n}F_{e}^{-N_{e}p^{\prime}}\pi_{e}^{-1}A_{r+C_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})\Big).

Proof.

For any
\boldsymbol{m}\in f^{k}(\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}Z_{ji})\bigcap G_{n,r}
we have

\displaystyle(g^{N_{e}})^{n}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\boldsymbol{m})\in A_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m}),\quad(g^{N_{e}})^{n}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}m_{jik})\in A_{r}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik}).

Since
Z_{ji}
has a product structure, then
f^{k}(\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}Z_{ji})
is close to a product structure in
\mathcal{M}
. So it is an intersection of families of stable and unstable disks, where each stable disk
\gamma^{s}
and each unstable disk
\gamma^{u}
intersect exactly at one point in
f^{k}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}Z_{ji}
. However, the angles between unstable and stable disks are not uniformly bounded away from
0
. Then there is
z\in Z_{ji}
such that
\boldsymbol{m}\in f^{k}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}\gamma^{u}(z)
and
m_{jik}\in f^{k}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}\gamma^{s}(z)
. Then by Lemma
7
,

\displaystyle\diam\big(\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}\gamma^{u}(z)\big)\leq C\beta^{p^{\prime}N_{e}},\quad\diam\big(\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}\gamma^{s}(z)\big)\leq C\beta^{p^{\prime}N_{e}}.

By Assumption
1
we have

\displaystyle\diam\big(f^{k}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}\gamma^{u}(z)\big)\leq C^{1+\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}},\quad\diam\big(f^{k}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}\gamma^{s}(z)\big)\leq C^{1+\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}.

Therefore,
d(\boldsymbol{m},m_{jik})\leq C_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}
for some
C_{\alpha}>0
. Having this estimate, we can compare
A_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})
and
A_{r}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})
.

Claim
:
A_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\subseteq A_{r+C_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})
.

For each
x\in A_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})
there exists
0\leq i\leq N_{e}-1
, such that

\displaystyle x\in g^{i}\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\big(B_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\big)\subseteq g^{i}\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\big(B_{r+C_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\big),

which means that
x\in A_{r+C_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})
. Hence, this claim holds.

Claim
:
Z_{ji}\bigcap F_{e}^{-N_{e}p^{\prime}}\pi_{e}^{-1}f^{-k}G_{n,r}\subseteq Z_{ji}\bigcap F_{e}^{-N_{e}p^{\prime}}\pi_{e}^{-1}f^{-k}\pi\pi_{X}^{-1}(g^{N_{e}})^{-n}A_{r+C_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})
.

For any
z\in Z_{ji}\bigcap F_{e}^{-N_{e}p^{\prime}}\pi_{e}^{-1}f^{-k}G_{n,r}
we have
f^{k}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}z\in f^{k}(\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}Z_{ji})\bigcap G_{n,r}
, and

\displaystyle(g^{N_{e}})^{n}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}f^{k}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}z)\in A_{r}(\pi_{\partial}f^{k}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}z)\subseteq A_{r+C_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik}),

i.e.,
z\in Z_{ji}\bigcap F_{e}^{-N_{e}p^{\prime}}\pi_{e}^{-1}f^{-k}\pi\pi_{X}^{-1}(g^{N_{e}})^{-n}A_{r+C_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})
. So, this claim holds.

Using the claims above, Lemma
17
, the relations
f_{*}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}=\mu_{\mathcal{M}}
and
(F_{e}^{N_{e}})_{*}\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}=\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}
we can estimate

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(f^{k}X_{i}\bigcap G_{n,r})

\displaystyle=\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(X_{i}\bigcap f^{-k}G_{n,r})=\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}(\pi_{e}^{-1}X_{i}\bigcap\pi_{e}^{-1}f^{-k}G_{n,r})

\displaystyle=\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}(F_{e}^{-N_{e}p^{\prime}}\pi_{e}^{-1}X_{i}\bigcap F_{e}^{-N_{e}p^{\prime}}\pi_{e}^{-1}f^{-k}G_{n,r})

\displaystyle=\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}(\bigcup_{j}Z_{ji}\bigcap F_{e}^{-N_{e}p^{\prime}}\pi_{e}^{-1}f^{-k}G_{n,r})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j}\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}\Big(Z_{ji}\bigcap F_{e}^{-N_{e}p^{\prime}}\pi_{e}^{-1}f^{-k}\pi\pi_{X}^{-1}(g^{N_{e}})^{-n}A_{r+C_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})\Big).

Since
\pi_{e}F_{e}^{p^{\prime}N_{e}}Z_{ji}\subseteq X_{i}
and
k<R^{\prime}_{i}
, then
\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}f^{k}=\pi_{X}F^{k}\pi^{-1}
is an identity map on
\pi_{e}F_{e}^{p^{\prime}N_{e}}Z_{ji}
. Using this we can continue our estimate above as

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j}\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}\Big(Z_{ji}\bigcap F_{e}^{-N_{e}p^{\prime}}\pi_{e}^{-1}(g^{N_{e}})^{-n}A_{r+C_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j}\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}\Big(Z_{ji}\bigcap F_{e}^{-N_{e}n}F_{e}^{-N_{e}p^{\prime}}\pi_{e}^{-1}A_{r+C_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})\Big).

Thus the lemma is proved.
∎

In order to proceed with further estimates we need to study
F_{e}^{-N_{e}p^{\prime}}\pi_{e}^{-1}A_{r+C_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})
. Define a family of sets
(Z^{\prime})\subseteq\mathcal{Q}_{2p^{\prime}}
such that

\displaystyle\bigcup Z^{\prime}\supseteq F_{e}^{-N_{e}p^{\prime}}\pi_{e}^{-1}A_{r+C_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik}),

(7.2)

and each
Z^{\prime}
in this family satisfies to

\displaystyle Z^{\prime}\bigcap F_{e}^{-N_{e}p^{\prime}}\pi_{e}^{-1}A_{r+C_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})\neq\emptyset.

By Lemma
7
, and by definitions of
Z^{\prime}
and
A_{r+C_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})
, we have
\diam\big(\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}Z^{\prime}\big)\leq C\beta^{p^{\prime}N_{e}}
, and there exists the smallest integer
k^{\prime}\in[0,N_{e})
, which depends on
Z^{\prime}
, such that

\displaystyle g^{-k^{\prime}}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}Z^{\prime}\bigcap\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\big(B_{r+C_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\big)\neq\emptyset,

that is,
\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}Z^{\prime}\bigcap\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\big(B_{r+C_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\big)\neq\emptyset
.

For each
X_{i^{\prime}}
, we collect all
Z^{\prime}
, e.g.,
\bigcup_{j^{\prime}}Z_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}}^{\prime}
such that
\bigcup_{j^{\prime}}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}}\subseteq X_{i^{\prime}}
. Then for each
k^{\prime}\in[0,N_{e})
, we collect
Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}}
from
(Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}})_{j^{\prime}}
, say,
Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}k^{\prime}}
, such that

\displaystyle\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\bigcap\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\big(B_{r+C_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\big)\neq\emptyset.

Hence, for each fixed
i^{\prime}
and
k^{\prime}\in[0,N_{e})
, there exists a set of all such allowable
j^{\prime}
, which we denote by
S(i^{\prime},k^{\prime})
. Therefore,
\bigcup_{j^{\prime}}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}}=\bigcup_{k^{\prime}=0}^{N_{e}-1}\bigcup_{j^{\prime}\in S(i^{\prime},k^{\prime})}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}k^{\prime}}
. Let a function
K_{i^{\prime}}:\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}\bigcup_{j^{\prime}\in S(i^{\prime},k^{\prime})}Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{N}_{0}
be defined as

\displaystyle K_{i^{\prime}}(x):

\displaystyle=\min\{n\geq 0:x\in\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\text{ for some }j^{\prime}\in S(i^{\prime},k^{\prime})\text{ such that }

\displaystyle F^{n}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\bigcap\pi^{-1}\big(B_{r+C_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\big)\neq\emptyset\}.

Clearly,
F^{K_{i^{\prime}}}:\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}\bigcup_{j^{\prime}\in S(i^{\prime},k^{\prime})}Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\to\Delta\bigcap(X_{i^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{N})
is an injective function, and pushes
\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}\bigcup_{j^{\prime}\in S(i^{\prime},k^{\prime})}Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}k^{\prime}}
up to
\Delta
, so that it intersect
\pi^{-1}\Big(B_{r+C_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\Big)
. With these preparations, we can now estimate
\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}(\bigcup Z^{\prime})
.

Lemma 19

\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}(\bigcup Z^{\prime})\precsim r+C^{\prime}_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}
for some
C_{\alpha}^{\prime}>0
.

Proof.

Now using
(\pi_{e})_{*}\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}=\mu_{X},(F^{N_{e}}_{e})_{*}\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}=\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}
and
g_{*}\mu_{X}=\mu_{X}
, we have

\displaystyle\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}(\bigcup Z^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}(F_{e}^{-N_{e}p^{\prime}}\pi_{e}^{-1}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}\bigcup Z^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\mu_{X}(\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}\bigcup Z^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i^{\prime}}\mu_{X}(\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}\bigcup_{j^{\prime}}Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i^{\prime}}\sum_{k^{\prime}}\mu_{X}(\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}\bigcup_{j^{\prime}\in S(i^{\prime},k^{\prime})}Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}k^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i^{\prime}}\sum_{k^{\prime}}\mu_{X}(g^{-k^{\prime}}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}}\bigcup_{j^{\prime}\in S(i^{\prime},k^{\prime})}Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}k^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i^{\prime}}\sum_{k^{\prime}}\mu_{X}(\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}\bigcup_{j^{\prime}\in S(i^{\prime},k^{\prime})}Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}k^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\precsim\sum_{i^{\prime}}\sum_{k^{\prime}}\mu_{\Delta}(\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}\bigcup_{j^{\prime}\in S(i^{\prime},k^{\prime})}Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}k^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\precsim\sum_{i^{\prime}}\sum_{k^{\prime}}\mu_{\Delta}\Big(F^{K_{i^{\prime}}}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}\bigcup_{j^{\prime}\in S(i^{\prime},k^{\prime})}Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\Big),

(7.3)

where“
\precsim
” in (
7.3
) is due to the injectivity and the measure of
\Delta
.

Claim
: There is a constant
C_{\alpha}^{\prime}>0
such that
F^{K_{i^{\prime}}}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}\bigcup_{j^{\prime}\in S(i^{\prime},k^{\prime})}Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\subseteq\big(X_{i^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{N}_{0}\big)\bigcap\pi^{-1}\big(B_{r+C^{\prime}_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\big)
.

For any
x\in\bigcup_{j^{\prime}\in S(i^{\prime},k^{\prime})}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}k^{\prime}}
, by the definition of
K_{i^{\prime}}
, there is
j^{\prime}\in S(i^{\prime},k^{\prime})
such that
x\in\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}k^{\prime}}
and

F^{K_{i^{\prime}}(x)}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\bigcap\pi^{-1}\big(B_{r+C_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\big)\neq\emptyset.

For any
z,y\in Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}k^{\prime}}
there is
o\in Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}k^{\prime}}
such that
z\in\gamma^{u}(o)
, and
y\in\gamma^{s}(o)
. By Definition
4
and Assumption
1
we have

\displaystyle\diam\big(\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}\gamma^{s}(o)\big)\leq C\beta^{N_{e}p^{\prime}-k^{\prime}},\quad\diam\big(\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}\gamma^{u}(o)\big)\leq C\beta^{N_{e}p^{\prime}+k^{\prime}},

\displaystyle d(f^{K_{i^{\prime}}(x)}\pi_{e}

\displaystyle F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}z,f^{K_{i^{\prime}}(x)}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}y)

\displaystyle\leq\diam\big(f^{K_{i^{\prime}}(x)}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}\gamma^{u}(o)\big)+\diam\big(f^{K_{i^{\prime}}(x)}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}\gamma^{s}(o)\big)\leq 2C^{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha N_{e}p^{\prime}-\alpha N_{e}}.

We can now estimate the distance between
\pi F^{K_{i^{\prime}}(x)}x
and
B_{r+C_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]
as follows

\displaystyle\dist\big(\pi F^{K_{i^{\prime}}(x)}x,B_{r+C_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}

\displaystyle m_{jik})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\big)

\displaystyle\leq\diam\big(\pi F^{K_{i^{\prime}}(x)}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\big)

\displaystyle=\diam\big(f^{K_{i^{\prime}}(x)}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\big)\leq 2^{\alpha}C^{1+\alpha}\beta^{\alpha N_{e}p^{\prime}-\alpha N_{e}}.

Therefore,

\pi F^{K_{i^{\prime}}}\bigcup_{j^{\prime}\in S(i^{\prime},k^{\prime})}\pi_{e}F_{e}^{N_{e}p^{\prime}-k^{\prime}}Z^{\prime}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\subseteq B_{r+C^{\prime}_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2],

where
C_{\alpha}^{\prime}:=2^{\alpha}C^{1+\alpha}\beta^{-\alpha N_{e}}+C_{\alpha}
. Hence. the claim holds.

Now, using the claims above and the relation
\Delta\bigcap\bigcup_{i^{\prime}}(X_{i^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{N}_{0})=\Delta
, we can continue an estimate of (
7.3
) as

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i^{\prime}}\sum_{k^{\prime}}\mu_{\Delta}\big[\big(X_{i^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{N}_{0}\big)\bigcap\pi^{-1}\big(B_{r+C^{\prime}_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\big)\big]

\displaystyle\leq N_{e}\sum_{i^{\prime}}\mu_{\Delta}\big[\big(X_{i^{\prime}}\times\mathbb{N}_{0}\big)\bigcap\pi^{-1}\big(B_{r+C^{\prime}_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\big)\big]

\displaystyle\precsim\mu_{\Delta}\big[\pi^{-1}\big(B_{r+C^{\prime}_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\big)\big]

\displaystyle\precsim\mu_{\mathcal{M}}\big(B_{r+C^{\prime}_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\big)\precsim r+C^{\prime}_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}},

where the last
``\precsim"
holds because
\frac{d\mu_{\mathcal{M}}}{d\Leb_{\mathcal{M}}}\in L^{\infty}
, and because
\partial Q
has an uniformly bounded curvature.
∎

We choose
p^{\prime}=n/4
, and estimate now
\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(G_{n,r})
.

Lemma 20

\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(G_{n,r})\leq C^{\prime}\beta^{n/2}+C^{\prime}(r+C^{\prime}_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha N_{e}n/4})
for some
C^{\prime}>0
.

Proof.

By (
7.2
) and Lemma
18
we have

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(G_{n,r})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\sum_{k<R_{i}^{\prime}}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(f^{k}X_{i}\bigcap G_{n,r})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i}\sum_{k<R_{i}^{\prime}}\sum_{j}\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}\Big(Z_{ji}\bigcap F_{e}^{-N_{e}n}F_{e}^{-N_{e}p^{\prime}}\pi_{e}^{-1}A_{r+C_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}}(\pi_{\partial}m_{jik})\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i}\sum_{k<R_{i}^{\prime}}\sum_{j}\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}\Big(Z_{ji}\bigcap F_{e}^{-N_{e}n}\bigcup Z^{\prime}\Big)-\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}(Z_{ji})\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}(\bigcup Z^{\prime})+\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}(Z_{ji})\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}(\bigcup Z^{\prime})

\displaystyle\precsim\sum_{i}\sum_{k<R_{i}^{\prime}}\sum_{j}\beta^{n-2p^{\prime}}\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}(Z_{ji})+\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}(Z_{ji})(r+C^{\prime}_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}}),

where the last
``\precsim"
is due to (
5.6
) and Lemma
19
.

By making use of Lemma
17
,
(\pi_{e})_{*}\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}=\mu_{X}
,
\int Rd\mu_{X}<\infty
and
(F_{e}^{N_{e}})_{*}\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}=\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}
, we can now continue the estimate above as

\displaystyle\precsim\sum_{i}\sum_{k<R_{i}^{\prime}}\beta^{n-2p^{\prime}}\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}(F_{e}^{-N_{e}p^{\prime}}\pi_{e}^{-1}X_{i})+\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}(F_{e}^{-N_{e}p^{\prime}}\pi_{e}^{-1}X_{i})(r+C^{\prime}_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}})

\displaystyle\precsim\sum_{i}\sum_{k<R_{i}^{\prime}}\beta^{n-2p^{\prime}}\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}(\pi_{e}^{-1}X_{i})+\mu_{\Delta_{e}^{\prime}}(\pi_{e}^{-1}X_{i})(r+C^{\prime}_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}})

\displaystyle\precsim\sum_{i}\sum_{k<R_{i}^{\prime}}\beta^{n-2p^{\prime}}\mu_{X}(X_{i})+\mu_{X}(X_{i})(r+C^{\prime}_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}})

\displaystyle\precsim\sum_{i}R_{i}^{\prime}\beta^{n-2p^{\prime}}\mu_{X}(X_{i})+R_{i}^{\prime}\mu_{X}(X_{i})(r+C^{\prime}_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}})

\displaystyle\precsim\int Rd\mu_{X}\beta^{n-2p^{\prime}}+\int Rd\mu_{X}(r+C^{\prime}_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha p^{\prime}N_{e}})\precsim\beta^{n/2}+r+C^{\prime}_{\alpha}\beta^{\alpha N_{e}n/4},

which concludes a proof of this lemma.
∎

Now we can estimate the range of
\mathcal{Z}_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})
for a particular
r>0
.

Lemma 21

Let any
\delta>0
be sufficiently small. Then for a.e.
\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathcal{M}
, there exists
N_{\boldsymbol{m}}\in\mathbb{N}
such that for any
n>N_{e}N_{\boldsymbol{m}}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\lfloor n/N_{e}\rfloor^{-(1+\delta)}}(\boldsymbol{m})\in\{1,2,\cdots,N_{e}N_{\boldsymbol{m}}-N_{e}\}\bigcup(n-N_{e},\infty).

Proof.

Let
r=n^{-(1+\delta)}
. By Lemma
20
we have

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(G_{n,n^{-(1+\delta)}})\precsim\beta^{n/2}+(n^{-(1+\delta)}+C_{\alpha}^{\prime}\beta^{\alpha N_{e}n/4})\precsim n^{-1-\delta}.

By Borel-Cantelli lemma, for a.e.
\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathcal{M}
there exists
N_{\boldsymbol{m}}>1
such that for any
n>N_{\boldsymbol{m}}
,

\displaystyle(g^{N_{e}})^{n}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\boldsymbol{m})\notin\bigcup_{0\leq i\leq N_{e}-1}g^{i}\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\big(B_{n^{-(1+\delta)}}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\big),

which means that for any
n>N_{e}N_{\boldsymbol{m}}
,

\displaystyle g^{n-N_{e}}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\boldsymbol{m})\notin\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\big(B_{\lfloor n/N_{e}\rfloor^{-(1+\delta)}}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\big).

Furthermore, for any
n\geq m>N_{e}N_{\boldsymbol{m}}
,

\displaystyle g^{m-N_{e}}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\boldsymbol{m})\notin\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\big(B_{\lfloor n/N_{e}\rfloor^{-(1+\delta)}}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\big).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\lfloor n/N_{e}\rfloor^{-(1+\delta)}}(\boldsymbol{m})

\displaystyle=\min\{m\geq 1:g^{m}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\boldsymbol{m})\in\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\big(B_{\lfloor n/N_{e}\rfloor^{-(1+\delta)}}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\big)\}

\displaystyle\in\{1,2,\cdots,N_{e}N_{\boldsymbol{m}}-N_{e}\}\bigcup(n-N_{e},\infty).

Thus the lemma is proved.
∎

To rule out the set
\{1,2,\cdots,N_{e}N_{\boldsymbol{m}}-N_{e}\}
, we need the following lemma.

Lemma 22

(Aperiodicity)

For a.e.
\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathcal{M}
and for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
g^{k}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\boldsymbol{m})\notin\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}(\{\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m}\}\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2])
.

Proof.

For any
q\in\partial Q
, let
\bar{A}_{0}(q):=\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}(\{q\}\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2])
. If
q
is a periodic point, then there exists
x\in\bar{A}_{0}(q)
such that there is
k\geq 1
satisfying
g^{k}(x)\in\bar{A}_{0}(q)
. Therefore,

\displaystyle g(x)\in g(\bar{A}_{0}(q)),\quad g(x)\in g^{-(k-1)}(\bar{A}_{0}(q)).

If
k>1
, then by Assumption
1
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{g(x)}g\big(\bar{A}_{0}(q)\big)\subseteq(Df^{i})\mathcal{T}(\{q\}\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2])\subseteq\interior C^{u}\text{ for some }i\geq 1,

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{g(x)}g^{-(k-1)}\big(\bar{A}_{0}(q)\big)\subseteq(Df^{-j})\mathcal{T}(\{q\}\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2])\subseteq\interior C^{s}\text{ for some }j\geq 1.

If
k=1
, then by Assumption
1
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{g(x)}g\big(\bar{A}_{0}(q)\big)\subseteq(Df^{i})\mathcal{T}(\{q\}\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2])\subseteq\interior C^{u}\text{ for some }i\geq 1,

and there exists
j\geq 0
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{g(x)}g^{-(k-1)}\big(\bar{A}_{0}(q)\big)\subseteq(Df^{-j})\mathcal{T}(\{q\}\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2])\subseteq\begin{cases}\interior C^{s},&\text{if }j>0\\
\mathcal{T}(\{q\}\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]),&\text{if }j=0\\
\end{cases}.

Then by Assumption
1
,
\dim\big\{\mathcal{T}_{g(x)}g\big(\bar{A}_{0}(q)\big)\bigcap\mathcal{T}_{g(x)}g^{-(k-1)}\big(\bar{A}_{0}(q)\big)\big\}<1
. On the other hand, since
\bar{A}_{0}(q)
is a countable union of one-dimensional connected submanifolds, such are as well
g\big(\bar{A}_{0}(q)\big)
and
g^{-(k-1)}\big(\bar{A}_{0}(q)\big)
. Their intersection is a union of countably-many points. Thus,

\displaystyle x\in g^{-1}\big[g\big(\bar{A}_{0}(q)\big)\bigcap g^{-(k-1)}\big(\bar{A}_{0}(q)\big)\big]

belongs to a union of countably-many points contained in
\bar{A}_{0}(q)
. Therefore, for any
q\in\partial Q

\displaystyle\Leb_{\bar{A}_{0}(q)}\{x\in X:g^{k}x\in\bar{A}_{0}(q)\text{ for some }k\geq 1\}=0,

where
\Leb_{\bar{A}_{0}(q)}
is the Lebesgue measure conditioned on the submanifold
\bar{A}_{0}(q)
. The same notations will be used below.

By lifting it to
\Delta
we have

\displaystyle\Leb_{\pi^{-1}(\{q\}\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2])}\{x\in\Delta:g^{k}(\pi_{X}x)\in\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}(\{q\}\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2])\text{ for some }k\geq 1\}=0.

Since
\pi
is an isomorphism, then

\displaystyle\Leb_{\{q\}\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]}\{\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathcal{M}:g^{k}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\boldsymbol{m})\in\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}(\{q\}\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2])\text{ for some }k\geq 1\}=0.

By Fubini’s theorem,

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathcal{M}}\{\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathcal{M}:g^{k}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\boldsymbol{m})\in\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}(\{\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m}\}\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2])\text{ for some }k\geq 1\}

\displaystyle\precsim\Leb_{\mathcal{M}}\{\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathcal{M}:g^{k}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\boldsymbol{m})\in\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}(\{\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m}\}\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2])\text{ for some }k\geq 1\}

\displaystyle\precsim\int_{\partial Q}\Leb_{\{q\}\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]}\{\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathcal{M}:g^{k}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\boldsymbol{m})\in\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}(\{\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m}\}\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2])\text{ for some }k\geq 1\}dq

\displaystyle\precsim\int_{\partial Q}\Leb_{\{q\}\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]}\{\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathcal{M}:g^{k}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\boldsymbol{m})\in\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}(\{q\}\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2])\text{ for some }k\geq 1\}dq=0,

which concludes a proof of this lemma.
∎

Now we can proceed to proving the main result of this subsection.

Lemma 23

For a.e.
\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathcal{M}
,

\displaystyle\liminf_{r\to 0}\frac{\log\mathcal{Z}_{2r}(\boldsymbol{m})}{-\log\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})}\geq 1,

where
S_{r}
is the section contained in the quasi-section
B_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]
.

Proof.

From Lemma
22
for a.e.
\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathcal{M}
we have that for any
k=1,2,\cdots,N_{e}N_{\boldsymbol{m}}-N_{e}
, and any
j<R(g^{k}\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\boldsymbol{m})
,

\displaystyle\pi F^{j}(g^{k}\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\boldsymbol{m})\notin\{\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m}\}\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2].

If it is not the case, then there is
j<R(g^{k}\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\boldsymbol{m})
such that

\displaystyle F^{j}(g^{k}\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\boldsymbol{m})\in\pi^{-1}(\{\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m}\}\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]),

which implies that
g^{k}\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\boldsymbol{m}\in\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}(\{\{\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m}\}\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2])
. But it is in contradiction with Lemma
22
. Choose now a small
r_{\boldsymbol{m}}>0
such that for any
r\in(0,r_{\boldsymbol{m}})
, any
k=1,2,\cdots,N_{e}N_{\boldsymbol{m}}-N_{e}
, and any
j<R(g^{k}\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\boldsymbol{m})
,

\displaystyle\pi F^{j}(g^{k}\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\boldsymbol{m})\notin B_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2].

This implies that for any
k=1,2,\cdots,N_{e}N_{\boldsymbol{m}}-N_{e}
, and for any
r\in(0,r_{\boldsymbol{m}})

\displaystyle g^{k}\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\boldsymbol{m}\notin\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\big(B_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\big).

Furthermore, for any
\delta>0
, and any
k=1,2,\cdots,N_{e}N_{\boldsymbol{m}}-N_{e}
, if
n>N_{e}r_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{-1/(1+\delta)}
, then

\displaystyle g^{k}\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\boldsymbol{m}\notin\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\big(B_{\lfloor n/N_{e}\rfloor^{-(1+\delta)}}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\big).

It follows from Lemma
21
that for any
n>\max\{N_{e}N_{\boldsymbol{m}},N_{e}r_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{-1/(1+\delta)}\}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\lfloor n/N_{e}\rfloor^{-(1+\delta)}}(\boldsymbol{m})

\displaystyle=\min\Big\{m\geq 1:g^{m}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\boldsymbol{m})\in\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\Big(B_{\lfloor n/N_{e}\rfloor^{-(1+\delta)}}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\Big)\Big\}

\displaystyle>n-N_{e}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\to\infty}\frac{\log\mathcal{Z}_{\lfloor n/N_{e}\rfloor^{-(1+\delta)}}(\boldsymbol{m})}{\log n}\geq 1.

Then for any sufficiently small
r\in(0,r_{\boldsymbol{m}})
such that
r\in(\lfloor(1+n)/N_{e}\rfloor^{-(1+\delta)},\lfloor n/N_{e}\rfloor^{-(1+\delta)})
for a sufficienly large
n>\max\{N_{e}N_{\boldsymbol{m}},N_{e}r_{\boldsymbol{m}}^{-1/(1+\delta)}\}
, we have
\mathcal{Z}_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})\geq n-N_{e}
, and

\displaystyle\liminf_{r\to 0}\frac{\log\mathcal{Z}_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})}{\log r^{-1/(1+\delta)}}\geq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\frac{\log(n-N_{e})}{\log(n+1)/N_{e}}=1

\displaystyle\implies\liminf_{r\to 0}\frac{\log\mathcal{Z}_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})}{-\log r}\geq 1/(1+\delta).

Note that
B_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]
is a quasi-section, and if
r>0
is sufficiently small, then, because
\partial Q
has a uniformly bounded curvature and
\frac{d\mu_{\mathcal{M}}}{d\Leb_{\mathcal{M}}}\in L^{\infty}
, we have

\displaystyle\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})\approx_{m}\mu_{\Delta}(\pi^{-1}S_{r})\approx(\pi_{*}\mu_{\Delta})\big(B_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\big)\approx r.

Then

\displaystyle\liminf_{r\to 0}\frac{\log\mathcal{Z}_{2r}(\boldsymbol{m})}{-\log\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})}

\displaystyle=\liminf_{r\to 0}\frac{\log\mathcal{Z}_{2r}(\boldsymbol{m})}{-\log\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{2r})}\frac{{\log\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{2r})}}{{\log\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})}}

\displaystyle=\liminf_{r\to 0}\frac{\log\mathcal{Z}_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})}{-\log r}\geq 1/(1+\delta).

By letting now
\delta\to 0
we conclude a proof of this lemma.
∎

7.2
Short returns on
X

In this subsection, we will prove short returns on
X
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to 0}\frac{1}{\mu_{X}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r})}\int\mathbbm{1}_{\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r}}\mathbbm{1}_{\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq pN_{e,m}-1}(f^{R})^{-j}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r})}d\mu_{X}=0.

Recall that
S_{r}:=S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})
is a section in
B_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]
. Let

\displaystyle\Lambda_{r,pN_{e,m}}(\boldsymbol{m}):=\{y\in S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m}):\text{ there exists }

\displaystyle i\geq 0,\text{ such that }y,f(y)\cdots f^{i}(y)\text{ visit }

\displaystyle X\text{ at most }pN_{e,m}\text{ times and }f^{i}(y)\in S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})\}.

Since
\Lambda_{r,pN_{e,m}}(\boldsymbol{m})\subseteq S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})
, then
\mu_{\mathcal{M}}\{\Lambda_{r,pN_{e,m}}(\boldsymbol{m})\}\approx\mu_{X}\{\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\Lambda_{r,pN_{e,m}}(\boldsymbol{m})\}
. Now we have the following lemma.

Lemma 24

For any
\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathcal{M}
,

\displaystyle\Lambda_{r,pN_{e,m}}(\boldsymbol{m})\subseteq S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})\bigcap\{y\in\mathcal{M}:\mathcal{Z}_{2r}(y)\leq pN_{e,m}\},

\displaystyle\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})\bigcap{\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq pN_{e,m}-1}g^{-j}\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})}\subseteq\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\Lambda_{r,pN_{e,m}}(\boldsymbol{m}),

where we recall that
\mathcal{Z}_{r}(\boldsymbol{m}):=\min\{n\geq 1:g^{n}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\boldsymbol{m})\in\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\big(B_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\big)\}
.

Proof.

Let
x\in\Lambda_{r,pN_{e,m}}(\boldsymbol{m})
, then
x\in S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})
, and there is
i\in\mathbb{N}
such that
x,f(x),\cdots f^{i}(x)
visit
X
at most
pN_{e,m}
times and
f^{i}(x)\in S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})
. Then
\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}x,\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}f(x),\cdots\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}f^{i}(x)
visit
X
at most
pN_{e,m}
or
pN_{e,m}+1
times. Thus,

\displaystyle x,f^{i}(x)\in S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})\subseteq B_{2r}(\pi_{\partial}x)\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2],

\displaystyle\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}x,\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}f^{i}(x)\in\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}[B_{2r}(\pi_{\partial}x)\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]].

Then
\mathcal{Z}_{2r}(x)\leq pN_{e,m}
, that is,
\Lambda_{r,pN_{e,m}}(\boldsymbol{m})\subseteq S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})\bigcap\{y\in\mathcal{M}:\mathcal{Z}_{2r}(y)\leq pN_{e,m}\}
.

Now let
x\in\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})
,
g^{j}x\in\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})
for some
j\in[1,pN_{e,m}-1]
. Then there are
k_{1}<R(x)
,
k_{2}<R(g^{j}x)
, such that
y:=F^{k_{1}}(x)\in\pi^{-1}S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})
and
F^{k_{2}}g^{j}(x)\in\pi^{-1}S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})
. So, there is
i\in\mathbb{N}
such that
y,Fy,\cdots,F^{i}y
visit
X
at most
pN_{e,m}
times and
F^{i}y\in\pi^{-1}S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})
, i.e.,
\pi y\in\Lambda_{r,pN_{e,m}}(\boldsymbol{m})
and
x=\pi_{X}y\in\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\Lambda_{r,pN_{e,m}}(\boldsymbol{m})
. Therefore, we prove

\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})\bigcap{\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq pN_{e,m}-1}g^{-j}\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})}\subseteq\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\Lambda_{r,pN_{e,m}}(\boldsymbol{m}),

which concludes the proof.∎

Now we can prove the main result of this subsection.

Lemma 25

For
\Leb_{\partial Q}
-a.e.
q\in\partial Q
(and
\boldsymbol{m}\in\pi_{\partial}^{-1}\{q\}
)

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to 0}\frac{1}{\mu_{X}\big(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r}\big)}\int_{\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r}}\mathbbm{1}_{\bigcup_{j=1}^{pN_{e,m}-1}g^{-j}\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r}}d\mu_{X}=0.

Proof.

Recall that
B_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]
is a quasi-section,
S_{r}=S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})
is a section, and
\Lambda_{r,pN_{e,m}}(\boldsymbol{m})\subseteq S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})
. By using these and Lemma
24
we have

\displaystyle\mu_{X}\Big\{\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})

\displaystyle\bigcap{\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq pN_{e,m}-1}g^{-j}\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{X}(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}\Lambda_{r,pN_{e,m}}(\boldsymbol{m}))

\displaystyle\precsim\mu_{\Delta}(\pi^{-1}\Lambda_{r,pN_{e,m}}(\boldsymbol{m}))

\displaystyle\precsim\mu_{\mathcal{M}}\big(\Lambda_{r,pN_{e,m}}(\boldsymbol{m})\big)

\displaystyle\precsim\mu_{\mathcal{M}}\Big[S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})\bigcap\{y\in\mathcal{M}:\mathcal{Z}_{2r}(y)\leq pN_{e,m}\}\Big]

\displaystyle\precsim\mu_{\mathcal{M}}\Big[\{B_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\}\bigcap\{y\in\mathcal{M}:\mathcal{Z}_{2r}(y)\leq pN_{e,m}\}\Big].

Besides, for any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, there is
C_{\epsilon}>0
such that

\displaystyle p=n^{1-\epsilon}=C_{\epsilon}^{\pm 1}\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})^{-1+\epsilon}.

Now we can continue the estimate above as

\displaystyle\precsim\mu_{\mathcal{M}}\Big[\{B_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\}\bigcap\{y\in\mathcal{M}:\mathcal{Z}_{2r}(y)\leq C_{\epsilon}\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})^{-1+\epsilon}N_{e,m}\}\Big]

\displaystyle\precsim\mu_{\mathcal{M}}\Big[\{B_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\}\bigcap\{y\in\mathcal{M}:\frac{-C_{\epsilon,m}+\log\mathcal{Z}_{2r}(y)}{-\log\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r})}\leq 1-\epsilon\}\Big]

\displaystyle\precsim\mu_{\mathcal{M}}\Big[\{B_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]\}\bigcap\{y\in\mathcal{M}:\inf_{r^{\prime}<r_{0}}\frac{-C_{\epsilon,m}+\log\mathcal{Z}_{2r^{\prime}}(y)}{-\log\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r^{\prime}})}\leq 1-\epsilon\}\Big]

(7.4)

where
C_{\epsilon,m}=\log C_{\epsilon}+\log N_{e,m}
and any
r_{0}>r
.

Before proceeding to estimating (
7.4
), we will need some preparations. By Lemma
23
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{r_{0}\to 0}\mu_{\mathcal{M}}\Big\{y\in\mathcal{M}:\inf_{r^{\prime}<r_{0}}\frac{-C_{\epsilon,m}+\log\mathcal{Z}_{2r^{\prime}}(y)}{-\log\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r^{\prime}})}\leq 1-\epsilon\Big\}=0.

(7.5)

Let
h:=\frac{d\mu_{\mathcal{M}}}{d\Leb_{\mathcal{M}}}
be the density of
\mu_{\mathcal{M}}
, and
\Leb_{[-\pi/2,\pi/2]}
, and
\Leb_{\partial Q}
are the Lebesgue measures on
[-\pi/2,\pi/2]
and
\partial Q
, respectively. For any
q\in\partial Q
, define now a measure
\mu_{q}

\displaystyle\mu_{q}(A):=\int_{A}h(q,\cdot)d\Leb_{[-\pi/2,\pi/2]}

(7.6)

for any measurable
A\subseteq[-\pi/2,\pi/2]
. Let also

\displaystyle U_{q,r_{0}}:=\big\{v\in[-\pi/2,\pi/2]:\inf_{r^{\prime}<r_{0}}\frac{-C_{\epsilon,m}+\log\mathcal{Z}_{2r^{\prime}}(q,v)}{-\log\mu_{\Delta_{m}}(\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r^{\prime}})}\leq 1-\epsilon\big\}.

Then (
7.5
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle\lim_{r_{0}\to 0}\int_{\partial Q}\mu_{q}(U_{q,r_{0}})d\Leb_{\partial Q}=0.

For any
\delta>0
let
T_{r_{0},\delta}:=\{q\in\partial Q:\mu_{q}(U_{q,r_{0}})>\delta\}
. Then
\lim_{r_{0}\to 0}\Leb_{\partial Q}(T_{r_{0},\delta})=0
. Now, using the Lebesgue differentiation theorem (which holds also on
\partial Q
), for any
r_{0},\delta>0
, there is a full measure set
Q_{r_{0},\delta}
in
\partial Q
such that for a.e.
q\in Q_{r_{0},\delta}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to 0}\frac{1}{\Leb_{\partial Q}\big(B_{r}(q)\big)}\int_{B_{r}(q)}\mathbbm{1}_{T_{r_{0},\delta}}d\Leb_{\partial Q}=\mathbbm{1}_{T_{r_{0},\delta}}(q).

Hence, if
q\notin T_{r_{0},\delta}
, then

\displaystyle\mu_{q}(U_{q,r_{0}})<\delta,\quad\int_{B_{r}(q)}\mathbbm{1}_{T_{r_{0},\delta}}d\Leb_{\partial Q}=o\big(\Leb_{\partial Q}\big\{B_{r}(q)\big\}\big).

Now we choose
r_{0}=1/n,\delta=1/k
. Then

\displaystyle\Leb_{\partial Q}(\bigcap_{n}T_{1/n,1/k})=0,\quad\Leb_{\partial Q}(\bigcup_{k}\bigcap_{n}T_{1/n,1/k})=0.

Choose
\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m}\in\bigcap_{n,k}Q_{1/n,1/k}\bigcap(\bigcap_{k}\bigcup_{n}T^{c}_{1/n,1/k})
, which has a full measure. Then for any
k\gg 1
there is
n_{k,\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m}}\in\mathbb{N}
such that for any
n\geq n_{k,\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m}}
,
\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m}\notin T_{1/n,1/k}
. (Here we used that
T_{1/t,1/k}\supseteq T_{1/(1+t),1/k}
for any
t\geq 1
).

For any
k\gg 1
, and any sufficiently small
r\in(0,1)
, there is
n\geq n_{k,\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m}}
such that
r\in[\frac{1}{n+1},\frac{1}{n})
. We choose
r_{0}=n^{-1}
, and continue the estimates of (
7.4
) as

\displaystyle\precsim\mu_{\mathcal{M}}\{(q,v)\in\mathcal{M}:q\in B_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m}),v\in U_{q,1/n}\}

\displaystyle\precsim\int_{B_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})}\mu_{q}(U_{q,1/n})d\Leb_{\partial Q}

\displaystyle\precsim\int_{B_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\bigcap T_{1/n,1/k}}\mu_{q}(U_{q,1/n})d\Leb_{\partial Q}+\int_{B_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\bigcap T^{c}_{1/n,1/k}}\mu_{q}(U_{q,1/n})d\Leb_{\partial Q}

\displaystyle\precsim\int_{B_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})}\mathbbm{1}_{T_{1/n,1/k}}d\Leb_{\partial Q}+\int_{B_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\bigcap T^{c}_{1/n,1/k}}k^{-1}d\Leb_{\partial Q}

\displaystyle=o\big(\Leb_{\partial Q}\big\{B_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\big\}\big)+O\big(\Leb_{\partial Q}\big\{B_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\big\}\big)k^{-1},

which means that

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to 0}\frac{\mu_{X}\Big\{\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})\bigcap{\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq pN_{e,m}}g^{-j}\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})}\Big\}}{\Leb_{\partial Q}\big\{B_{r}(\pi_{\partial}\boldsymbol{m})\big\}}=O(1/k),

does not depend on
n
anymore. Let
k\to\infty
.
Then for any
q\in\bigcap_{n,k}Q_{1/n,1/k}\bigcap(\bigcap_{k}\bigcup_{n}T^{c}_{1/n,1/k})
,

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to 0}\frac{\mu_{X}\Big\{\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})\bigcap{\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq pN_{e,m}}g^{-j}\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})}\Big\}}{\Leb_{\partial Q}\big\{B_{r}(q)\big\}}=0.

Finally, because
B_{r}(q)\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2]
is a quasi-section, we have that if
r>0
is small enough, then
\mu_{X}\big(\pi_{X}\pi^{-1}S_{r}(\boldsymbol{m})\big)\approx\mu_{\mathcal{M}}(B_{r}(q)\times[-\pi/2,\pi/2])\approx r\approx\Leb_{\partial Q}\big\{B_{r}(q)\big\}
. It concludes a proof of this lemma.
∎

8
Conclusion of a proof of Theorem
2.1

Recall that (
6.3
) is already proved. Then it follows from Lemma
15
that, what is required, is to estimate (
6.2
). In this section several technical lemmas are dedicated to a proof of (
6.2
), followed by a proof of Theorem
2.1
at the end of this section.

From (
6.2
) and throughout this section,
n\approx r^{-1},p:=n^{1-\epsilon}
, and
\epsilon\in(0,1)
is sufficiently small number in Lemma
15
. Notice that, in Lemma
15
,
n
also depends on any fixed bounded intervals
J_{1},J_{2}\cdots,J_{k^{\prime}}
in
\mathbb{R}^{+}\bigcup\{0\}
. Since they are fixed, from now on we drop them and write
n\approx r^{-1}
only. Now consider

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq l\leq n-p}\sup_{\boldsymbol{a}\geq 1}\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{0}=\boldsymbol{a}}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n-l})\Big]-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{0}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n-l})\Big|.

Since there are finitely many
\boldsymbol{a}\geq 1
, we just need to estimate

\displaystyle n\cdot\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{0}=\boldsymbol{a}}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n-l})\Big]-\mathbb{E}\mathbbm{1}_{\boldsymbol{X}_{0}=\boldsymbol{a}}\mathbb{E}h(\boldsymbol{X}_{p},\cdots,\boldsymbol{X}_{n-l})\Big|

To simplify notations we set throughout this section

\displaystyle T=F^{R_{m}},\quad U=F_{e,m}^{N_{e,m}},\quad H=\pi_{\Delta_{m}}\pi^{-1}S_{r}.

\hbox to170.72pt{\vbox to55.59pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 85.8195pt\lower-25.67558pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-44.43048pt}{-15.66309pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
